The silence counts from the run's start and from each line Claude Code writes since (a step, a tool's result, a word of the reply): a run that keeps working is never ended by it. The limit counts from the run's start, the time the run waits for the owner included (an OK on an action in a connected app, a login), and not the time it waited to start. A Chief's turn has the same two. An app that does not know `"idle"` draws the run's `problem`, as for any run that failed.
- **After the end** what waits for that bot starts, the owner's oldest message first, as after any run (see A message that waits). Not after a sign-out: while Claude is signed out what waits stays waiting, and starts with the sign-in (see A job's line).

**A message that waits**, when `features` has `message-waits` ([ADR 011](adr/011-a-bot-does-one-thing-at-a-time-and-the-owners-message-waits.md)). A bot is busy while a run of it is going, whoever started it, a run that waits for the owner's OK included (`running: true`). A message to a busy bot starts a run beside the ones going, up to three runs of one bot at once ([ADR 062](adr/062-a-message-to-a-busy-bot-starts-beside-the-run-that-is-going.md)): the owner's message, a Board tile's request, a run the Chief asks for, a teammate's message, notes that wake it, a turn in a group, and the answers it waited for once the run that asked has ended. Other work (a job, a call, a run that carries on after an OK, the memory review) starts only when the bot is free. A message of the owner to a bot that has three runs going is not refused: it is kept on the botdot server, on disk, with its files, and starts by itself as soon as one of them ends, before anything else that waits for that bot and in the order sent. A run that starts beside others is told what they were asked, and that it can't see them. The fields are named `queued`; `waiting` means other things (a bot's `state`, the feed's event for notes, a Chief chat's plan).

- **A waiting run.** `POST /api/bots/:id/run` to a busy bot answers `201` with `{ id, botId, status: "queued", prompt, attachments, queuedAt }`. `id` is the id its run will have: every later answer about that message, and the run itself, carries it. `attachments` is a list, empty without files; `queuedAt` is when it was sent. `prompt` is what its run will have: the message, or the bot's main assignment for a request without one, then with `customPrompt: false`. It also has `botName` and `trigger`, and `startedBy: "chief"` when the Chief asked. To a free bot the answer is the run, as before. The same `key` sent again answers that run, or that waiting run, with `again: true` (see A message sent twice).
- **Too many.** At most ten of the owner's messages wait for one bot. One more is refused: `429 { error: "<Bot> already has 10 of your messages waiting. Take one back, or wait." }`. Nothing is kept and its key is not taken, so the same message can be sent again later. Keep it in the message box, as a send that failed.
- **The run's record while it waits.** `GET /api/runs/:id` answers the waiting run above, and `GET /api/runs/:id/wait` holds for it as for a run that is going and answers the waiting run when its time is up. A waiting run is not in `GET /api/runs`: it has no record until it starts. When it starts it is a run like any other (`run_started` on the feed), with `sentAt`, when the owner sent it.
- **The list.** Each bot in `GET /api/bots`, and `GET /api/bots/:id`, has `queued`: the owner's messages that wait, oldest first, `[]` when none. An entry is `{ id, text, attachments, at }`: `id` as above, `text` as the owner wrote it (`""` for files alone), `attachments` as a run has them (a list, empty without files), `at` when it was sent. A request a Board tile made of the bot is in the list, with the tile's message as `text`; a run the Chief asked for is not.
- **The feed.** `queued` with `{ chat: "bot:<id>", botId, queued }`, the whole list, whenever it changes: a message is taken, starts, or is taken back. A message that starts leaves the list in the moment its `run_started` comes.
- **Take back.** `DELETE /api/bots/:id/queued/:queuedId` answers `200 { text, attachments }`, for the message box, and the message is gone: it starts nothing, its id is no run, and its key may be sent again. Once its run exists the answer is `409 { error: "It has already started.", runId }`: show that run. `404` for an id the bot does not know. Only the owner may call it; the Chief's tools get `403`.
- **A file that is gone.** Files are checked when the message is sent, as for a run: `400` with the reason. A file that is gone from the workspace when the message's turn comes is left out, and the message starts without it.
- **A message that cannot start** when its turn comes is a run with `status: "error"` and the reason in `error`, under the id it was answered with.
- **After a restart** of botdot the run that was going is `interrupted`, and what waited starts, once, in the same order.
- **A Board tile** that asks a busy bot answers `runId` and `queued: true` (see The Board): its request waits among the owner's messages.
- **The Chief's `run_bot`** on a busy bot gets the waiting run, with `note: "<Bot> is busy: this starts when it is free."`, and its chip in the chat reads `Asked <Bot> · starts when it is free`. Such a run waits with the other work, behind the owner's messages, and is refused with `429` only when ten of that other work wait. `wait_for_run` waits for it through its start to its end, and says that it has not started yet when its time is up.
- **For the rules of What follows a yes** a waiting message counts as a message the owner sent the bot from when it was sent, also while it waits.
- A server without `message-waits` starts a second run beside the first for a message to a working bot.

**A quick side question** ([ADR 063](adr/063-a-btw-question-is-answered-without-tools-beside-the-run.md)). The owner's `POST /api/bots/:id/run` whose `prompt` starts with `/btw` (any case, then a space or a line break), with no `attachments`, to a bot that has a run going, is answered by a talk-only turn of Claude: no tools, no MCP servers, no session, told what each run going now was asked, its working line, plan and reply draft. It answers `201` with a run of its own, `status: "running"` and `btw: true`, whose `prompt` is what the owner wrote and whose `result` is the answer; it follows like any run (the feed's `run_started` and `run_finished`, `GET /api/runs/:runId/stream`) and ends in seconds, or with `status: "error"` within two minutes. It takes none of the three places of the runs going and never waits (it is no part of `queued`, and has no Stop). Draw it as any exchange of the chat. To a bot with no run going, with files, or with nothing after `/btw`, it is a message as any, and `/btw` is dropped from what the bot is sent unless nothing follows it. The Chief's `run_bot` has no `/btw`.

**Steer**, when `features` has `steer` ([ADR 040](adr/040-a-waiting-message-is-steered-into-the-run-at-its-next-tool-call.md)). Under each of a bot's waiting messages the apps show "Steer" beside "Take back". A press hands that message to the run that is going: the run reads it at its next tool call and goes on in the same run, with the message in its record. Without a press the message waits as above.
- **The press.** `POST /api/bots/:id/queued/:queuedId/steer` answers `200 { queued }`, the bot's waiting messages as they are now, that one with `steering: true`; the feed's `queued` follows. Pressed again: the same answer, steered once. The message stays a waiting message until the run reads it: Take back still answers its text and files, and the run never sees it. `409 { error }` in the server's words when the run that is going can't take it, and the message keeps waiting: a run the Chief asked for ("<Bot> is working on a request of the Chief's. This starts when it is free."), a teammate's message or the answers it waited for ("<Bot> is answering a teammate. …"), a turn in a group, a run without tools (the memory review, a new bot's first message) or a bot that is not working. `409 { error: "It has already started.", runId }` once it was read or started as its own run, `runId` being the run that has it; `404` for an id the bot does not know. Only the owner may call it; the Chief's tools get `403`.
- **The list.** An entry of `queued` that was steered and not read yet has `steering: true`. The apps show "Steering…" in place of "Waits until <Bot> is free", with Take back alone.
- **When it is read.** The message leaves `queued` (the feed's `queued`) and is in the run's `steered`, a list in the order read, each `{ id, text, attachments, at, readAt }`: `id` the id it waited with, `at` when it was sent, `readAt` when the run read it. `steered` is on the run in `GET /api/runs`, `GET /api/runs/:runId` and the feed's run events, and the feed says `run_steered`. The apps draw each after the run's request, as the owner's message with "Steered" and its time, without buttons. Its key answers the run that read it (see A message sent twice).
- **Not read.** A run that makes no tool call after the press (it ends with its reply, or it is stopped) does not read it: the message loses `steering` and starts as its own run when the bot is free, in its order, once. After a restart of botdot the same.
- **For the rules of What follows a yes** a steered message counts as a message the owner sent the bot from `at`, as while it waited.
- A server without `steer` has no such route (`404`): show no button.

**The account's computer** ([ADR 035](adr/035-the-account-has-one-computer-all-its-bots-work-on-with-sudo.md), #104). Every bot with `computer: "linux"` works on the account's one computer, so a bot's computer is that computer:
- `GET /api/computers`: `{ <bot id>: "running" | "stopped" }` for every bot with a computer, each the account computer's state; `{}` while it was never made.
- `GET /api/bots/:id/computer` and its `status` and `logs` describe the account's computer.
- `POST /api/bots/:id/computer` with `{ action: "stop" }` answers `409` "The account's computer stays on: every bot with a computer works on it." and turns nothing off. `{ action: "reset" }` erases only the bot's old computer of its own, made before, never the account's, and answers `{ state }`, the account computer's state. `start` starts the account's computer and the bot's own screen on it. While the computer runs and the bot's screen is off (#134: its commands and files need none, and an unused screen ends after 30 minutes), `GET /api/bots/:id/computer` has `screenOff: { text, start }` in the server's words: the apps show `text` instead of the live screen, with a button reading `start` that sends `start`. Deleting a bot ends its screen there and erases its browser's profile (its logins); its folder `/workspace/<bot id>` and the computer stay. `DELETE /api/bots/:id`'s `computer` is about its old computer of its own: `"erased"` when it had one.
- The apps offer no "Turn off" and no "Keep its computer on" (#109): the computer is always on. A bot's `computerAlwaysOn` is still kept and taken from an older app: with it the account's computer is made at once rather than at a bot's first computer tool, and nothing else.

**What each bot uses of it** (#109), when `features` has `computer-usage`. `GET /api/bots/:id/computer` has `usage` while the account's computer runs (absent while it does not, or when it could not be measured): `{ text, bots: [{ id, name, memoryBytes, cpuPercent, processes, text }], other: { name, memoryBytes, cpuPercent, processes, text } }`. `bots` is every bot with a computer, most memory first; each counts the processes of its screen (its desktop, its browser, the programs started there) and of the commands it ran, by the screen in their environment or their parent's. `memoryBytes` is their resident memory (what processes share counts in each), `cpuPercent` their CPU over half a second, 100 being one CPU fully used. `other` is what runs on no bot's screen. Each `text` is its line in the server's words ("1.6 GB memory · 88% CPU", or "nothing running"), `usage.text` says what is counted, and `other.name` names that row: draw them as they are, a row per bot with its name. Nothing in it is the account's share or a limit. It takes about a second to measure.

**What survives, one reset, and an old computer's files** (#107), when `features` has `computer-reset` and `old-computer-copy`. `/home/box`, `/workspace` and what the bots installed with apt survive a stop and a start of the account's computer. `/home/box`, `/workspace`, the browsers' profiles (their logins) and the computer's Tailscale with its login (`/var/lib/botdot-tailscale`) are on volumes of the account's own: an update to a newer desktop keeps them, and loses what was installed with apt.
- `GET /api/bots/:id/computer` also has, in the server's words, which the apps draw and never write themselves:
  - `bots`: `[{ id, name }]`, every bot with a computer: those a reset or an update touches.
  - `reset`: `{ title, text, yes }`, the confirmation of a reset: `text` names those bots and what is lost ("It is erased for every bot that works on it: Coder and Scout. Lost: the files in /home/box and /workspace, the browsers' logins, its Tailscale login and every program installed on it. …").
  - `update`: `{ text, yes }`, only while `outdated`: what an update keeps and what it loses. Show it where the update is offered.
  - `old`: `{ state, folder, text, copy, erase, confirm }`, only while the bot still has an old computer of its own (`state` is `"stopped"`, or `"running"` for one botdot has not stopped yet). `text` says it is kept, stopped; `folder` is where its files are copied, `/workspace/<bot id>/old-computer`; `copy` and `erase` are what its two buttons read, and `confirm` is the confirmation of the erase.
- `POST /api/computer/reset` erases the account's computer for every bot: the computer, `/home/box`, `/workspace`, the browsers' logins, its Tailscale login and everything installed. Answers `{ state: "none", erased }`, `erased: false` when there was no computer. The next computer tool of any bot makes it again, fresh. A bot's old computer of its own is not touched. Show `reset` as a confirmation first. `409 { error }` while a bot with a computer is in a run ("Wait until Scout finishes its run: it works on the computer.").
- `POST /api/computer/update` makes the account's computer again from the desktop botdot has now, for every bot, keeping its volumes. Answers `{ state, updated }`, with `error` when it failed: a computer made before the volumes has its folders copied onto them first, and if that fails nothing is changed. Show `update.text` as a confirmation first. The same `409` while a bot works.
- `POST /api/bots/:id/computer` with `{ action: "copy-old" }` copies the home of the bot's old computer of its own into `/workspace/<bot id>/old-computer` on the account's computer, which it starts, and answers `{ copied }`, that folder. The old computer stays as it is: `{ action: "reset" }` then erases it. `409` for a bot without one.
- **The Chief** cannot call these. Its `reset_computer` asks: `POST /api/computer/reset` from the Chief's tools answers `202 { held: true, id, say }` and leaves a card in Needs you, an ask of kind `"chief-action"` (see The Chief reads the web) with `title` "Reset the account's computer", `meta` the text of `reset`, no `rows`, and `text` "Asks to reset the account's computer". The owner's Approve on it does the reset, and the Chief is told in its chat. The update and the copy are the owner's alone (`403`).
- `POST /api/computer/restart` (#109) stops the account's computer and starts it again, for every bot: what runs there ends, every bot's screen with it, and its files, logins and installed programs are kept. A bot's screen starts again with its next computer tool. Answers `{ ok: true, state: "running" }`, or `{ ok: false, state, error }` with why it did not come up; a computer never made is made. From the Chief's `restart_computer` while a bot with a computer is in a run, it is not done: `202 { held: true, id, say }` and a card like the reset's, with `title` "Restart the account's computer", `meta` naming the bots at work and what ends and what is kept, and `text` "Asks to restart the account's computer"; the owner's Approve restarts it, under those runs. `POST /api/bots/:id/computer/restart` is the same, `409` for a bot without a computer. The apps offer no restart.

**A new bot's first message** (#511). Shortly after a bot is made, once nothing about the bots has changed for 20 seconds (so the jobs the Chief adds next are in it), botdot starts one short run of it with `trigger: "hello"`, unless it has a run or waiting work by then. The bot says what it does, when it runs next, what still waits for the owner's OK (held changes, permissions, jobs or job changes not yet approved), and at most one question; it has no computer, connectors or web (it may note a memory) and does not do its main assignment. Draw only its reply: its `prompt` is botdot's task, not the owner's words, so no bubble or chip stands above it. It counts as a new reply like any answer to the owner. It is not the Chief's message and does not count toward the Chief's three a day.

**Bots messaging bots.** A run has `trigger: "bot"` and `fromBot: { id, name, emoji }` when another bot's message started it. Show it as that bot's message, not yours; its `prompt` is the message. The apps fold such a run to two lines, "Message from Fleet Doctor ›" and "Replied to Fleet Doctor ›" (or "Messaged …" when it wrote to others), each bot named with its small head before its name, that open to the message, what it sent and its reply. `hop` and `chainId` tie a chain of hand-offs together. A priority message to a bot that already has 10 runs waiting goes to its inbox instead (`delivered: "inbox"`, and `full: true` in the sender's answer, `POST /api/self/session/message`; `answer: true` there says the message is the answer the other bot waits for, which its follow-up reads). A note wakes its bot as soon as it is free ([ADR 049](adr/049-a-note-to-a-bot-wakes-it-as-soon-as-it-is-free.md), feature `notes-wake`): an idle bot starts at once, a busy one when its run ends, unless a run that starts first reads the note. The answers a waiting bot collects wake nothing (its `trigger: "replies"` run reads them). A run its notes woke has `trigger: "bot"` and no `fromBot`: show only its `inbox` (the notes) and its reply, not its `prompt` (botdot's task, not the owner's words); it notifies nobody (`notificationAudience: "internal"`), and carries the `hop` and `chainId` of the latest note. A run with `trigger: "replies"` started because the teammates its bot asked have answered: show only its `inbox` (the answers) and its reply, not its `prompt`. `inbox` lists the notes other bots left that the run read at its start (`{ fromId, from, emoji, color, text, at }`). `GET /api/bots/:id/inbox` returns the notes still waiting for its next run, in the same shape (feature `inbox`); fetch it again on `bot_message` and on that bot's `run_started`. The apps show how long the oldest has waited (its `at`). `POST /api/bots/:id/inbox/run` is "Run now" under them (feature `notes-wake`): `{ status: "started" }`, or `"queued"` for a bot at work (with a full queue, the first run that waits reads them); 409 when no note waits or only answers its own question collects (its follow-up reads those), 404 for no such bot. That run is the owner's request (`notificationAudience: "owner"`). A run that messaged teammates has `sentToAll`, each bot once (`{ id, name, emoji, color, delivered, text }`, `text` being the latest it sent that bot), and `sentTo`, the latest of them. A bot with `mayMessageAll: true` (the default, for new and existing bots) may message every other bot, including bots made later. Otherwise `mayMessage` holds the bot ids it may message. Change it with `PUT /api/bots/:id`: `{ mayMessageAll: true }`, or `{ mayMessage: [...] }` to limit it (this turns `mayMessageAll` off). `othersMayAsk` ("Other bots may ask it", #136) is `true` unless the owner turned it off with `PUT /api/bots/:id` `{ othersMayAsk: false }` (or the Chief's `update_bot`): such a bot is left out of every other bot's teammates whatever their `mayMessage`, and a bot's `message_bot` to it, or its `ask_permission` to message it, is refused with "<name> is kept to the owner: other bots may not ask it (its setting "Other bots may ask it" is off)." It may still message others, and a bot it messaged may answer it. An app that gets no `othersMayAsk` from the server shows no switch for it. A bot's `list_teammates` (`POST /api/self/session/teammates`) gives each teammate's `reaches`, written by botdot: its connected apps by name (once Composio is connected), the names of the Vault items shared with it with their kind (`"Proton Mail (login)"`, never a value), `"computer"` and `"web"`.

## Groups

With `groups` in `features` ([ADR 018](adr/018-a-group-is-a-chat-of-its-own-and-a-turn-is-a-run-of-its-member.md)). A **group** is one chat with two to six bots, its members. It is a third kind of chat beside a bot's and a Chief chat, with the key `group:<id>` wherever a chat has a key. A bot can be in several groups, and its own chat is untouched by any of them. Against a server without the feature there are no groups: offer none.

The folder of bots in the list is a **category**. Its name in the API is older than the word: `group` on a bot, and `POST /api/groups/rename`. Neither has to do with a group.

**A group** is `{ id, name, members, category, pinned, order, createdAt, updatedAt }`:

- `members`: bot ids, in the group's order.
- `category`: the category it is listed in, as `group` on a bot names one (`""`: none).
- `pinned`: its place on the Pinned page (1 = first; 0 = not pinned). With `pins-shared` in `features` ([ADR 058](adr/058-pinned-bots-and-groups-share-one-order.md), #276) a bot's `pinned` and a group's count in one order: list everything pinned by `pinned`, bots and groups mixed. Pinning, moving, unpinning or deleting a bot or a group numbers both kinds 1, 2, 3… again; a bot and a group never share a place. A server without it counts a group's `pinned` among the pinned groups only: list the pinned groups after the pinned bots. An app from before it sorts each kind by `pinned` and still shows every pinned one, the bots first. On the first start with it, the pinned groups keep their place after the last pinned bot.
- `order`: its place among the groups of its category that are not pinned (1 = first; 0 while pinned). List a category's groups after its bots. A bot's own `order` does not count groups and does not change when a group moves.

**The routes** are the owner's. The Chief's tools may list the groups, make one, rename one and change its members, in a turn the owner started (see The Chief and groups); every other call of theirs is refused with `403`.

- `GET /api/group-chats`: every group. `GET /api/group-chats/:id`: one, or `404 { error: "Group not found" }`. Each has, beside its record, what its chat holds now (see A group's chat): `unread` and `firstUnread`, `last`, `working`, `asks` and `limit`.
- `POST /api/group-chats` with `{ name, members }`: `201` and the group. It is placed last among the groups of the category of the first bot given. Refused with `400` and a sentence for the owner, and nothing is kept: no name ("Give the group a name."), fewer than two bots ("A group has at least two bots."), more than six ("A group has at most six bots."), a bot that does not exist ("There is no bot "…"."), a bot named twice ("Coder is in the group twice.") and two bots that a mention could not tell apart ("Issue Writer and issue writer would both be @IssueWriter in the group. Rename one of them first.").
- `PUT /api/group-chats/:id` with any of `{ name, members, category, order, pinned }`: answers the group.
  - `members` is the whole list as it is to be: it adds a member, takes one out and changes their order, and is checked as for a new group. Taking a member out of a group of two is refused ("A group has at least two bots. Add another, or delete the group.").
  - `category`, `order` and `pinned` move it as `group`, `order` and `pinned` move a bot (`PUT /api/bots/:id`): a number puts it at that place and the other groups (for `pinned`, the other pinned bots and groups) make room, `pinned: true` pins it last, `pinned: 0` unpins it; a group moved to another category without an `order` goes last there.
- `DELETE /api/group-chats/:id`: `{ ok: true, name }`. The group, its read position, the owner's messages in it and the files they attached there are gone. No bot and no run is deleted: its members' turns stay their own past runs.
- `POST /api/groups/rename` with `{ from, to }` renames a category, or removes it with `to: ""`: its bots and the groups in it move with it, in their order. Answers `{ ok: true, moved }`, the number of bots.

### The Chief and groups

The Chief of Staff makes a group when the owner asks it to, by chat ([Milestone 10](product/milestone10.md), capability 18). It goes through the routes above, as the Chief (its key, and `X-Chief-Chat`), and is refused what they refuse, with the same sentence.

| The Chief's tool | Calls | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `list_groups` | `GET /api/group-chats` | every turn of a chat, not a look |
| `create_group` (`name`, `bots`) | `POST /api/group-chats` with `{ name, members }` | a turn the owner started |
| `update_group` (`group`, and `name`, `add`, `remove`) | `GET /api/group-chats`, then `PUT /api/group-chats/:id` with `{ name?, members? }` | a turn the owner started |

- **What the Chief may call:** `GET /api/group-chats`, `GET /api/group-chats/:id`, `POST /api/group-chats` and `PUT /api/group-chats/:id`. Nothing else of a group: `DELETE /api/group-chats/:id`, every route of a group's chat (`/chat`, `/messages`, `/stop`, `/go-on`) and `POST /api/uploads?to=group:<id>` answer `403 { error: "The Chief's tools can't do that; only the owner can." }`. The Chief has no tool that deletes a group, writes in one, stops one or lets one go on.
- **Only a turn the owner started makes or changes a group,** as for the Chief's memory (see The Chief's memory): the tools' server offers `create_group` and `update_group` only when it is told the turn is the owner's, and `POST` and `PUT` answer `403 { error }` to the Chief's call from any other turn: one botdot started (a late result, an answered card, a tool request), a look, a chat where no reply is going, or no chat at all. Nothing is made or changed. Reading the groups is not bound to the turn.
- **What the Chief is handed** is `{ id, name, members: [{ id, name }], category }` for a group, in the list and alone: the members with their bots' names, and nothing of the group's chat (no `last`, `asks`, `unread`, `working` or `limit`). The owner's apps are answered as before.
- **What it sends:** `name` and `members` only. `category`, `order` and `pinned` in the Chief's call are ignored: a group's place in the list is the owner's. A `PUT` with neither is `400 { error: "Say what to change: the group's name, or the bots to add or take out." }`. A member is a bot's id, so the Chief is never one: `"chief"` is refused as a bot that does not exist. The tools take a bot by the name the owner says, or its id, and a group by its name or its id, and look the ids up; a name no bot has, or that several have, makes and changes nothing.
- **The answer** to its `POST` and `PUT` has, beside the group, `say`, the sentence for the Chief to pass on, and where something changed `chip`, both written by the botdot server from the group as it was stored: "Made the group Dev: Coder, Reviewer and Issue Writer", "Renamed the group Dev to Team", "Added Cook to Team", "Took Issue Writer out of Team", "Changed the order in Team", several joined with " · ". A call that changed nothing has `say` ("Nothing changed: the group Team has Coder and Reviewer.") and no `chip`.
- **The chip** is an action of the Chief's reply, `{ kind: "group", text, groupId }`: print `text` whole. An app that does not know the kind prints the same `text`. While one of these tools runs, the `activity` of the stream has the server's `text` ("Reading the groups", "Making a group", "Changing a group").
- **Every app has the group at once:** the Chief's change is said on the feed as `groups`, like the owner's own.

**A deleted bot** leaves every group it was in. A group stays with one member left, or with none, with its chat, until the owner deletes it.

**On the feed** a change to a group is `groups`, with no data: read the groups again. An app from before groups knows no such event.

**A turn** is a run of a member in a group. Its run has `groupId` and `groupMessageId`, the owner's message in the group that it belongs to, also while it waits to start, when it never started, and when it carries on after the owner's yes (`continues`).

- **It is no part of its bot's own chat.** `GET /api/runs` without `group`, for one bot or for all, never holds a turn. A turn is not counted in the bot's `unread`, is never its `firstUnread`, its open question (`asks`) or the reason for a `failed` face, is no entry of the bot's `queued` while it waits, and no hit of `GET /api/search` unless the app asks for groups.
- `GET /api/runs?groups`: every bot's runs, those of their own chats and their turns in every group, newest first, without the passes, with `botId`, `limit` and `before` as for a bot's runs. Settings → Activity is drawn from it.
- `GET /api/runs?group=:id`: the turns of that group, newest first, without the passes, with `botId`, `limit` and `before` as for a bot's runs. `GET /api/runs/:runId` answers a turn like any run, a pass with `passed: true`. A deleted group's turns keep their `groupId`.
- **On the feed** a turn is announced as `group_run_started` and `group_run_finished`, its line as `group_opening_line` and, with `working-line` in `features`, `group_working_line` (see The working line), and its wait for an OK as `group_run_waits`: the data of `run_started`, `run_finished`, `opening_line`, `working_line` and `run_waits`, with `groupId` and with `chat: "group:<id>"`. No `unread`, `asks` or `queued` of the bot follows from a turn. With a turn's start and its end the server also sends `bots`: the member is `running` in `GET /api/bots` meanwhile, as for any of its runs.
- **The chat's key.** `POST /api/read` takes `"group:<id>"` as a chat and keeps its read position; a group that does not exist is refused like any chat that does not. It sends no `chat_seen` for a group, and `POST /api/presence` takes none: both are for apps from before `unread`, which know no group. A message's `key` is kept per chat, a group's under `group:<id>`.

### A group's chat

The owner writes once and the botdot server gives the turns ([ADR 019](adr/019-the-botdot-server-gives-the-turns-in-a-group.md)). No app starts a turn, and no bot starts another's.

**Who has a turn.** Without a mention every member has one, in the group's order. With mentions only the members named have one, in the group's order whatever the order in the text, each once; a mention of a member's job names that member. A mention of a bot that is no member names nobody. A paused member (a bot with jobs that are all switched off), a deleted one and one that has left the group are skipped, also when its turn already waited for it: it gets no turn and leaves no reply. One turn of a message is at work at a time: a turn starts when the one before it has ended. Two messages have two lines of turns, which can be at work side by side.

**"@all"** (#124, when `features` has `group-all`). A message whose text starts with `@all`, in any case, then a space, a line or its end, is for every member at once. Every member that is not paused, deleted or out of the group gets one turn, all given at the same moment; a member that is busy has its turn wait in its own queue, which holds up none of the others, and it starts when that member is free, beside the others. Each turn is asked with the text without `@all`, and is told that the others answer at the same time and to hand nothing on: a reply to such a message names nobody into a turn, whatever it mentions. Mentions after `@all` pick nobody: `to` is every member. The message keeps its text as written, `@all` included, and has `together: true`. Its working members show as any (`working`), and "Stop all" ends them all. Each reply counts towards the limit as any. In a group's chat `@all` is no member's mention: a member named "All" cannot be mentioned there. Typing `@` at the start of a message in a group offers `@all` first, before the members.

**A turn** is a run of its member (see above), told who the members are, whose message or whose hand-on gave it the turn, when to answer, pass, hand on and end, and to be short. It is handed the owner's message and the group's chat as the owner saw it, every line with who wrote it ([ADR 020](adr/020-a-member-reads-the-groups-chat-and-keeps-its-own-memory.md)): no pass, and nothing a forget left out. It is handed nothing of the bot's own chat, takes no note from the bot's inbox and answers no question of the bot's own. Its notes and its lookup are the bot's own, and the turn is one of the bot's past runs.

- **A reply** is a turn that ended with something to show: an answer, or a failure (`error`, `stopped`, `interrupted`, each with its `problem` and, when someone ended it, `endedBy`). After a turn that failed the next member's turn starts.
- **A pass** is a turn that ended well with the one word `PASS` as its whole reply. Its run has `passed: true` and `quiet: true`. It is in no chat, no preview, no count of new replies and no search hit, in the group and in the bot's own chat. Never draw it.
- **A hand-on** is a reply that names a member as a mention is written (`@Coder`): the botdot server gives that member the next turn of the same message, after the turns that wait already. Two members named have a turn each, in the group's order. Nobody has a turn from a reply that names nobody, a bot that is no member, or its own writer, and a member a turn already waits for in that line gets no second one. A name inside code hands nothing on: a span between two backticks and a block between two lines of three, where the apps draw no mention either. A pass, a failed and a stopped turn hand nothing on. A member for whom ten messages wait already cannot take a turn handed on: it is a failed reply under that member's name, whose `error` says so and who handed it on. The turn handed on is a reply in the group like any: its run has `handedBy`, `{ id, name, emoji, color }` of the member that named it, the `groupMessageId` and the `prompt` of the owner's message, and it counts as a run a teammate started: what it saves is not the owner's word ([ADR 020](adr/020-a-member-reads-the-groups-chat-and-keeps-its-own-memory.md)). No app starts it and none has to draw it differently.

**Sending.** `POST /api/group-chats/:id/messages` with `{ text, key?, attachments? }`: `201` and the message. `attachments`: files uploaded to this group (see The owner's files in a group); with files, `text` may be empty, and the message's `text` is then `""`: its turns are asked to have a look at the attached files. `key`: see A message sent twice; it is kept under `group:<id>`, and the same key again answers `200` with the message as it is now and `again: true`. Refused, and nothing is kept: `400` for no text and no file ("Write a message first."), a text of more than 20,000 characters, a file that was not uploaded to this group, an ill-formed key, and a group whose bots are all gone ("Dev has no bots left. Add one, or delete the group."); `429` when ten of the owner's messages wait already for a member the message is for ("Coder already has 10 of your messages waiting. Take one back, or wait."), the ten of that bot's own chat (see A message that waits) counted with them.

**A message** is `{ id, text, at, attachments?, to, waitsFor, next, canTakeBack, atLimit?, stopped?, together? }`:

- `attachments`: the files the owner attached, `{ name, file, size }` each.
- `to`: the members it was for, in the group's order.
- `waitsFor`: the members whose turn waits for them, because they are working, in a group or in their own chat. Say that the message waits for them. Such a turn is the kind `turn` in that bot's queue: it starts as early as the owner's own message to that bot, is no entry of the bot's `queued`, and the members after it are not held up. It starts when its member is free and no other turn of the message is at work.
- `next`: the members whose turn has not come yet, in the order they will have it: the group's, with the member whose question was open first (see A card and a question in a group), then the members a reply handed the work on to. A member can be in `next` that is not in `to`, and one that already replied. While Claude is signed out no turn is given: every member is in `next` until the sign-in.
- `canTakeBack`: none of its turns has started.
- `atLimit: true`: its line stopped at the limit and waits for "Go on" (see The limit). Its `next` are the turns that wait, and `waitsFor` is empty.
- `stopped: true`: "Stop all" ended its line.
- `together: true`: it was sent with `@all`: every member's turn was given at once (see "@all").

**Taking back.** `DELETE /api/group-chats/:id/messages/:messageId`, while `canTakeBack`: `{ text, attachments }`, for the message box. The message is gone, its waiting turns start nothing, and its key is free again. `409 { error: "It has already started." }` when a turn of it has started, `404` when there is no such message. `410 { error }` when its line is over and no member took a turn, each being paused, deleted or out of the group as its turn came, or stopped while it waited: nothing of it started, the message stays in the chat, and `error` is the sentence to show ("It is over, and nobody took a turn on it: its members were paused or gone, or you stopped their turns. Send it again when one can answer."). `POST /api/runs/:runId/stop` on one waiting turn takes that turn alone: its message stays.

**"Stop all".** `POST /api/group-chats/:id/stop`: `{ ok: true, stopped, dropped }`, the runs ended and the turns that now never start. Every run of the group that is at work is ended by the owner (`status: "stopped"`, `endedBy: "owner"`), and each message one of whose turns had started loses the turns that wait and those to come (`stopped: true`). A message none of whose turns had started is untouched: it starts when its members are free. `POST /api/runs/:runId/stop` still ends one run, and its line goes on with the next member.

**The limit.** After 12 replies to one message since the owner wrote it, the next turn of that message does not start: its line stops, and the group's chat says so. A pass is no reply and is not counted; a failed and a stopped reply are, and so is the reply of a run that carried on after the owner's yes. A line that ends by itself at the twelfth reply, with no turn left, does not stop: it is done.

- **The stopped line** is `limit` on the group, in `GET /api/group-chats`, `GET /api/group-chats/:id` and on its chat: `null`, or `{ at, replies, waits, messages, text }` while a line of the group is stopped.
  - `text`: the sentence to show, "The group stopped after 12 replies without you.". Draw it at the end of the chat, with a button "Go on".
  - `replies`: the limit, 12. `at`: when it stopped.
  - `waits`: the members whose turn waits, in the order they will have it.
  - `messages`: the ids of the messages whose lines are stopped, one as a rule; each has `atLimit: true`.
- **On the feed** each change is `group_limit`, `{ chat: "group:<id>", groupId, limit }`: a line stopped, went on, was ended by "Stop all", or who waits changed. The message follows as `group_message`. Nothing rings for it.
- **"Go on".** `POST /api/group-chats/:id/go-on`, with `{ messageId }` for one line or no body for every stopped line of the group: `200 { ok: true, wentOn: true, messages }`, the messages that go on as they are now. The turn that waited starts, and the count starts at one: 12 more replies. `409 { error: "The group is not stopped." }` when no line of the group, or not that one, is stopped at the limit.
- **Typed.** A message that is the words "Go on" and nothing else, in any case, alone or with the signs typed around them (a full stop, an exclamation mark, quotes, the signs of bold), sent with `POST /api/group-chats/:id/messages` while a line of the group is stopped, does the same and is no message: the answer is `200 { ok: true, wentOn: true, messages }`, without an `id`, and nothing is added to the chat. Empty the message box and show nothing for it. Its `key` is taken: sent again, it answers the first of those messages with `again: true`. With no line stopped, and with any other words, it is a message like any.
- **A new message of the owner's** starts a line of its own, counted from one. The stopped line stays stopped beside it until "Go on" or "Stop all".
- **What waits, waits.** A stopped line is kept across a restart and a signed-out Claude. When the members it waited for have all left the group or were deleted, it is over: `limit` is `null` and nothing goes on. A paused member that waits is skipped when the line goes on. A turn of it that waited for a busy member left that bot's queue as the line stopped and is in `next` again, first. "Stop all" drops the turns of a stopped line as it drops any that wait (`dropped`), and the line is over (`stopped: true`, `limit: null`).

**The chat.** `GET /api/group-chats/:id/chat?limit=50&before=<id>`: `{ items, more, working, asks, limit, cards, unread, firstUnread, readAt }`.

- `items`: the owner's messages and the replies, oldest first. A message is `{ type: "message", ...the message }`; a reply is `{ type: "reply", id, at, run }`, `run` being the member's run as `GET /api/runs/:runId` answers it (its `botId`, `botName`, `botColor`, `endedAt`, `status`, `result`, `problem`, `endedBy`, `files`) and `at` its start. A reply stands after the message it belongs to (`run.groupMessageId`), in the order the turns started. No pass and no turn at work is in it.
- Pages: `limit` items, the newest (50 when it is left out, 200 at most). `before` is the `id` of the oldest item the app holds: the page before it. `more` says that older items are left. An id that is no item answers no items.
- `working`: the members at work in the group, in the order they started: `{ botId, runId, messageId, startedAt, openingLine?, workingLine? }`. `openingLine`: see The opening line. `workingLine`, with `working-line` in `features`: what that member's line reads now (see The working line); a turn that has no line yet has no such field.
- `asks` and `cards`: the group's open question and the cards that belong to it (see A card and a question in a group).
- `limit`: the stopped line, or `null` (see The limit).
- `unread`, `firstUnread`, `readAt`: its new replies (below).

**New replies.** A group has one read position (`POST /api/read` with `"group:<id>"`, see New replies), so reading it in one app reads it in all. `unread` is the number of replies after it, whichever member wrote them, a failed or stopped one too, never a pass; `firstUnread` is the `id` of the first of them, the reply to draw the "New" line above. Both are on the group in `GET /api/group-chats` and on its chat. The feed says each change as `group_unread`, and a reply that lands while an app reports the group is read at once.

**No reply in a group notifies.** `group_run_finished` has `notify: false`: draw the reply, count it when it has `new: true`, and never ring for it. A reply that asks the owner something is the one exception (below).

**In the list** a group has `last`, its newest reply for the preview, `{ runId, botId, botName, status, at, text }` with `text` the reply's first 300 characters or, for one that failed, its `problem` (`null` while it has none), and `working`, the ids of the members at work in it. `group_run_started` and `group_run_finished` carry the same for the row: who works, and the newest reply with its `botName`.

**After a restart** a turn that waited starts, a run that was cut is a reply with `status: "interrupted"`, the line goes on with the next member, and no member has two turns on one message.

### A card and a question in a group

What a turn asks of the owner belongs to the group ([ADR 020](adr/020-a-member-reads-the-groups-chat-and-keeps-its-own-memory.md)).

**A card** asked in a turn has `groupId` and `groupName` beside its bot: an ask (`GET /api/asks`, and each entry of the list in the feed's `asks` event and in its first message), a login request (`GET /api/vault/requests`, `login_request`), a waiting memory change (`GET /api/memory/pending`, `approvals.memoryChanges`), and what waits of a job or a Board tile (`approvals.jobs`, `approvals.tiles`). Draw it in Needs you with its bot's name and the group's, and at the end of that group's chat; its notification opens the group. A card without `groupId` is its bot's own chat's, as before. A card of a group that was deleted has neither field.

- `cards` on the group's chat: the cards that belong to the group, oldest first, `{ kind, id, botId, runId, at }` each. `kind` says where the card is found whole, by its `id`: `ask`, `login`, `memory`, `job` (the job's id, of the bot `botId`) or `tile`. A guarded memory change is among them only for an app that asks with `?guarded=1`, as in its own list.
- **Only the owner answers a card,** with the routes every card has. No member's reply is an answer: "go ahead" from another member is a reply and changes nothing.
- **After the owner's yes** the member carries on in the group: the run that carries on has the `groupId` and `groupMessageId` of the turn that stopped and `continues`, and its reply is a reply in the group. The card's `then` and the answer's `carriesOn` are as for any card. Nothing carries on once the owner sent the group a later message for which that member had a turn, once the group is gone, or once the bot has left it; what the owner writes in the bot's own chat takes up nothing in a group, and the reverse.

**A question.** A member's reply that asks the owner something (answer buttons, or a card of questions: see A bot that asks) is the group's open question, not the bot's: `asks` on the group, in `GET /api/group-chats` and on its chat, is `{ botId, botName, text, runId, at }`, or `null`. Say "Asks" on the group's row. It is the newest such reply, while it is its member's newest reply in the group and the owner has not written to the group since; a reply of another member does not end it. Any message of the owner's to the group ends it, and taking that message back opens it again. The feed says each change as `group_asks`. The bot's own `asks`, and the feed's `asks` for that bot, are untouched by it.

- `group_run_finished` for such a reply has `asks: true`, `title` ("Coder asks") and `question`. It has `notify: true` when no app showed the group as it arrived: ring for it, and open the group. While an app reports the group (`POST /api/read` with `"group:<id>"`) the reply is read at once, the owner has the question before them, and the event has `notify: false` with `asks: true` still: no app rings, the one that shows it and any other. An app decides by `notify` alone, since it knows its own screen and not another app's.
- **The owner's next message without a mention gives the member that asked the first turn;** the others follow in the group's order. The message's `to` is in the group's order all the same; `next` is in the order of the turns. With a mention the turn is for whom it names.

### The owner's files in a group

`POST /api/uploads?to=group:<id>` takes a file for a message in that group, as `?to=bot:<id>` does for a bot (see Attaching files), and answers the same `{ name, file, size, readable }`. Send it in `attachments` with the message. The route is the owner's: the Chief's tools are refused with `403`. `400` for a group that does not exist.

The file is kept once, with the group, where no bot reads it. Each member whose turn on that message starts is handed a copy of its own in its workspace, and its run has that copy in `attachments`; a member that has no turn on the message, and a bot outside the group, are handed none. Later turns read in the group's chat that the message had files, by their names.

A file a member attaches to its reply is in `files` of that reply's run, as in a bot's own chat (see Files from bots). It reaches no other member's workspace.

## A bot that asks

With `asks-you` in `features` the botdot server says which bot asks the owner something. A reply asks when it ended well with answer buttons (`choices`) or a card of questions (`questions`) and addresses the owner: the answer to their message, a scheduled job's run, or a run another bot started whose reply asks the owner what only they can answer, such as their OK on a change (#233). When the asking bot waits for that reply, the questions go back to it as text instead (`askedAsker: true`, no `choices` or `questions`), and it asks the owner in its own chat (#310). Such a run has `notificationAudience: "owner"`, so it is new and notifies like the owner's own; a plain reply to a teammate stays `internal`. A run the Chief started never asks. A question written as plain prose is a reply like any other. An app draws what the server says and works none of it out; against a server without the feature the fields are missing.

- **A bot** (`GET /api/bots`, `GET /api/bots/:id`) has `asks: { text, runId, at }` while it has an open question, and no `asks` otherwise. The open question is that of the bot's newest reply to the owner, until the owner sends that bot a message. The Chief's tools are handed the open question of a left-out run (Memory, The contract) as `{ runId, at, leftOut: true }`, without `text`; the owner's apps are sent it whole.
  - `text` is the question in one line, cut after 200 characters with "…": for answer buttons the last sentence of the reply that ends in a question mark, or the reply's last line when no sentence does; for a card its first question, with ` (+2 more)` when there are more. `runId` is the run whose reply asks, and `at` when that reply reached the chat.
  - **It ends** when the owner sends that bot a message: typed, a tapped answer, or sent from a Board tile, also a message that waits its turn (see A message that waits). A waiting message that is taken back leaves the question open again. A newer reply to the owner with a question takes its place, and a newer one without ends it, a failed run included. "Run now" of a job is no message, and neither is a run the server started again after a yes.
  - Nothing is stored for it: it is read from the bot's runs, so it is the same after a restart.
- **The bot's `state`** is `needs_you` while it has an open question.
- **On the feed** the event `asks` with `{ botId, asks }` says each change, with `asks: null` when the question ended. It shares its name with the event that lists what waits in Needs you (`{ waiting, list }`): tell them apart by `botId`.
- **A run** has `asks: true` when its reply asks, in `GET /api/runs`, `GET /api/runs/:runId` and the feed's run summary, and with it `question`: the question in one line, worded as `text` above. Its `choices` or `questions` are on the run as before. A run that does not ask has neither field.
- **Its notification:** the run summary of such a run has `title`, "Mail Sorter asks", to show in place of the bot's name, and `question` to show under it. The summary's `result` is the reply's first 300 characters, and a long reply asks at its end: show `question`, and `result` only against a server that sends none. `result` is the question too when the reply is nothing but its buttons or its card. A tap opens the bot's chat, where the answers are.
- **A scheduled job's question is never quiet.** A scheduled run whose reply asks is delivered as a reply whatever the job's `notify`: `reporting: "delivered"`, no `quiet`, new when it arrives, and never taken for "nothing new". A report the run sent with `notify_owner` stands above the reply's own text. In quiet hours it is held as a report is (`reporting: "held"`, `quiet`, no `asks`, and the bot has no open question yet) unless that report was urgent, and on the first minute outside them it is delivered: a second `run_finished` with `new: true` and `asks: true`, and the bot's `asks`. A run of an earlier server that counted as quiet stays quiet: only a run delivered this way is taken out.
- **A run the Chief started** (`startedBy: "chief"`), and the run that finishes its task after the bot's teammates answered (`trigger: "replies"`), asks the owner nothing: it has no `choices`, no `questions` and no `asks`, and the bot gets no open question. Such a run is not told how to mark a question; when its reply marks one all the same, the questions and their answers stay in `result` as text ("Possible answers: Yes | No", or "Questions:" with one line each), which is what the Chief is handed. A run another bot started is as before: its `choices` are kept, and it has no `asks`.

## New replies

With `unread` in `features` the botdot server keeps how far the owner has read each chat and says what is new ([ADR 005](adr/005-read-positions-on-the-server.md)). An app draws what the server says and computes none of it; against a server without the feature the fields are missing, and nothing is new.

- **What counts.** A new reply is a reply after the chat's read position that is meant for the owner: a bot's answer to the owner, a scheduled report that reached the owner, the final reply of a one-time job's run (`oneTime: true`), a run that didn't finish (`error`, `stopped`, `interrupted`), and in a Chief chat the Chief's replies, its reminders and a bot's `report` message. A run another bot or the Chief started (the ones the apps fold), a `quiet` run and a memory review never count. A run is `quiet` (`quiet: true` on the run, and on the feed's `run_finished`) when it was scheduled, ended well and holds nothing for the owner: an AI run that reported nothing, or whose report was suppressed or is still held, and a script job's run whose output went to the AI (`handedOver: true` on the run), since the AI's report is what reaches the owner. With `job-line` in `features`, a scheduled job's failed run can be `quiet` too, and has its `problem` all the same (see A job's line). Leave a quiet run out of the chat, the bot list's preview and the notifications, whatever its `kind` or its `status`; it stays in the run history, with its `result`.
- **A bot** (`GET /api/bots`, `GET /api/bots/:id`) has `unread`, the number of new replies in its chat, not capped, and `firstUnread`, the `id` of the run that is the first of them in the chat, the one to draw the "New" line above (`null` when nothing is new).
- **A Chief chat** (each of `chats` in `GET /api/chief`, and `GET /api/chief/chats/:chatId`) has `unread`, and `firstUnread`, the place of the first new message in the chat's whole `messages` (0 is the first message; with `?limit` or `?offset` it is still counted from the start of the chat, like `offset`). `GET /api/chief` also has `unread`, the sum over all chats, for the Chief's one row.
- **Reporting the chat on screen:** `POST /api/read` with `{ "app": "<id>", "chat": "bot:<id>" }`, `"chief:<chatId>"`, `"group:<id>"` (with `groups` in `features`), or `null` for no chat. `app` is an id the app makes up for itself once (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, up to 80 characters; a random one per install or per start is fine); it only tells two apps apart. The call says what that app shows now and takes the place of its last report:
  - Send the chat when it is shown and the app is in front (the page visible and focused, the activity resumed, the window key), and again every `repeatMs` while that stays so.
  - Send `null` as soon as it is not: the chat is closed, the page is hidden, the app goes to the background.
  - With a chat it answers `{ ok: true, chat, readAt, repeatMs }` (`repeatMs` is 10000), with `null` `{ ok: true, chat: null, repeatMs }`.

  The chat is read up to now, and a reply that lands while an app reports it is read at once. For a group the feed's event is `group_unread` (see A group's chat). A reply after the app said `null`, or opened another chat, is new. A report that is not repeated runs out after 15 seconds, for an app that could not say it left. A chat that doesn't exist, or a report without `app`, is refused with `400` and nothing changes.
- **On the feed.** `unread` gives a chat's count and first new reply whenever the count changes, in every app within a second; a report that changes nothing sends nothing, and messages that reach a Chief chat together send one. `run_finished` and `chief_reply` carry `new: true` for a reply that counts as new; without it the reply was read at once or doesn't count. After `reset`, or when starting, read the counts from `GET /api/bots` and `GET /api/chief`.
- **Notifications** follow the same state: notify for an event with `new: true`, remove a chat's notification on `unread` with `unread: 0`. `chat_seen`, `webActive` and `POST /api/presence` are for apps from before this feature and are not part of it.
- On the first start of a botdot server with this feature every chat is read. The positions are part of botdot's data: a restart keeps them and a backup carries them. Deleting a bot, a Chief chat or a group removes its position.

## The opening line

With `opening-line` in `features`, a bot or the Chief may write one short line before work that uses tools ("Checking the weather in Brussels."), and the botdot server offers it while that run is going on. An app shows it under the bot's chat, with a pulsing dot in the bot's colour, and as the bot's preview in the list in place of "Working…"; a run without a line shows its steps as before. Against a server without the feature there is no line.

- **What it is.** The first text the bot writes before its run's first tool call, on one line, without Markdown's marks, its first 120 characters, without the full stop or ellipsis it ends with: draw it as it is and put your own ellipsis after it. It is plain text, never markup. Text with no tool call after it is the reply, and text after the first tool call is never a line, so the line appears with the first tool call. Every bot is told it may write one.
- **Where it is offered, for a bot's run:**
  - on the feed: `opening_line` with `{ chat: "bot:<botId>", botId, runId, text }`, once per run, when the line is known;
  - `openingLine` on a running run: in `GET /api/runs`, `GET /api/runs/:runId` and each of `running` in the feed's `hello`. A run without one has no such field;
  - `{ "type": "opening_line", "text" }` in `GET /api/runs/:runId/stream` (with and without `?simple`); a client that connects after the line was said gets it once what already happened was replayed.
- **For the Chief:**
  - on the feed: `opening_line` with `{ chat: "chief:<chatId>", chatId, text }`;
  - `openingLine` on a chat the Chief is replying in: in each of `chats` of `GET /api/chief` and in `GET /api/chief/chats/:chatId`;
  - `{ type: "opening_line", text }` in `GET /api/chief/chats/:chatId/stream`, again for a client that connects later.
- **When it goes.** The server keeps the line for the running run only. Remove it on that run's `run_finished` (same `id` as `runId`), whatever its `status` (a bot has one run going at a time: the next run's line comes with its own `opening_line`), and on the chat's `chief_reply` without `from` (the Chief's own reply; also when it failed or was stopped), or on the Chief stream's `done`. After `reset`, or when starting, read the fields above: a run that ended has no `openingLine`. The line is not in the stored run, in `result`, or in the chat's `messages`.
- **It is not a reply.** It never counts as new: no `unread`, no dot, no "New" line, and no notification, for a scheduled run either.

## The working line

With `working-line` in `features`, the botdot server says what the one line of a working bot reads, and changes it as the run goes on: "Checking the weather in Brussels", then "Reading nytimes.com", "Starting its computer", "Messaging Web Surfer". It is still one line with Stop. An app draws the text it is given, under the bot's chat and as the bot's preview in the list, and has no words of its own for a running run's step.

- **What it is.** One line of plain text, never markup: draw it as it is and put your own ellipsis after it. It has no ellipsis of its own. The server's words for a step are at most 80 characters, and an opening line is passed on as it was read, up to its 120: so a line is at most 120 characters. The server decides its words in one place (`lib/working-line.js`):
  - it starts with the run's opening line, when the bot wrote one (see The opening line): the same text as `opening_line`;
  - then the server's words for each step that follows, a tool call ("Reading nytimes.com", "Reading plan.md", "Check disk space", "Running a command", "Looking at its screen", "Searching the web", "Using gmail", "Saving a note", "Messaging Web Surfer") or the start of the bot's computer ("Starting its computer", while it starts; then the step it was started for again. Since #108 no start waits for a free computer: the account has one, and an older server's "Waiting for a free computer" is gone). The step that comes with the opening line is what that line announced, and does not take its place;
  - the run of a job that uses AI reads "Running <job's name>" from its start, until the bot says more. A script job has no line, because it has no run while its script runs: see below;
  - a step without words leaves the line as it was, and a run that uses no tools has no line at all: show "Working", as for a run without an opening line.
- **A command's words** (#232). For a command (Claude Code's `Bash`, and the computer's `run_shell`, `run_with_login`, `run_with_ssh_key`, `run_with_file` and `start_program`) the line reads the bot's own `description` of it ("Check disk space"), cut to 80 characters on one line; the bot is told it is shown to the owner. Without one it reads the tool's phrase ("Running a command", "Running a command on its computer", "Starting a program on its computer"). The other computer tools read plain words: "Looking at its screen", "Clicking", "Typing", "Pressing keys", "Scrolling", "Reading a file on its computer", "Saving a file on its computer", and "Using its computer" for the rest.
- **What it never holds.** A host name and not a whole address, a file's name and not its path, a command's description and never the command, and nothing that was typed, searched for or sent. The names in it (a host, a file, a skill, a connected app, a teammate, a job) are cut by the server.
- **Where it is offered,** for a bot's run in its own chat, through the same three places as the opening line:
  - on the feed: `working_line` with `{ chat: "bot:<botId>", botId, runId, text }`, each time the line changes. `text` is the whole line as it reads now;
  - `workingLine` on a running run: in `GET /api/runs`, `GET /api/runs/:runId` and each of `running` in the feed's `hello`. A run that has no line yet has no such field;
  - `{ "type": "working_line", "text" }` in `GET /api/runs/:runId/stream` (with and without `?simple`), each time the line changes; a client that connects later gets the line as it reads now, once what already happened was replayed and after the run's `opening_line`.
- **At most once a second.** The server sends a change of a run's line at most once a second, and always sends the last one: of several changes within a second only the newest is sent, when the second is over. The feed, the stream and `workingLine` change together: `workingLine` is the text that was sent last. An app draws each text when it comes and needs no limit of its own.
- **A script job has no line.** While the script of a script job runs the server holds no run of it: no `run_started`, no running run in `GET /api/runs` or in `hello`, and the bot is not `running` and its `state` is not `working`. So nothing is at work for an app, and neither app draws a line or Stop for it. Its run is made when the script has ended with something to show (output, a failure, or what it hands to the AI): then `run_finished` says it once, with `kind: "script"`, and a script that ended well and printed nothing leaves no run at all. When the script hands its output to the AI, that look is a run of the job like any other and reads "Running <job's name>". A script that is still going is said where its job is said: "Run now" answers `skipped` (see Jobs).
- **On a reconnect** nothing of a line is lost and no old line is drawn over a newer one. The feed keeps, for a replay, only the newest change of each run's line (see The live feed): an app that comes back with `Last-Event-ID` gets `hello`, whose `running` has each run's `workingLine` as it reads now, and then what it missed, in which a run's line comes once, with the text that was sent last. However long and however busily the bots worked meanwhile, their lines take one place each among the kept events.
- **`opening_line` is sent as before,** and `openingLine` stays on the run: an app that does not know the working line shows the opening line as it always did. An app that uses the working line reads the line of a bot's run from `working_line` and `workingLine` only, and ignores `opening_line` and `openingLine` for it: the working line's first text is the opening line, and an app that drew both could change the line twice within a second. One exception keeps an app working against a server that has the working line for a bot's own run only: a running run, or an entry of a group's `working`, that has `openingLine` and no `workingLine` shows its `openingLine`.
- **Without the feature** there is no `working_line` and no `workingLine`: show the opening line as before (`opening_line`, `openingLine`), and "Working" for a run without one.
- **When it goes.** As the opening line: the server keeps it for the running run only. Remove it on that run's `run_finished` (same `id` as `runId`), whatever its `status`: the reply, a failure and Stop all end it, and no `working_line` for a run comes after its `run_finished`. After `reset`, or when starting, read `workingLine` from the running runs: a run that ended has none. The line is not in the stored run, not in `result`, and its changes are not in the run's `events`: the run's log holds the tool calls the words are made from.
- **"Waiting for your OK" stays the app's:** while a run has `waitingFor: "ok"` (see An action in a connected app) the app shows those words in the line's place, with a still dot, and the working line again when the field goes.
- **It is not a reply.** It never counts as new: no `unread`, no dot, no "New" line, and no notification.
- **A turn in a group** has a working line as a run in a bot's own chat has, under the same `working-line` in `features`: the same words, the same limit of one change a second, and nothing of it in the turn's log. It is offered:
  - on the feed: `group_working_line` with `{ chat: "group:<id>", groupId, botId, runId, text }`, each time the line of that turn changes. `text` is the whole line as it reads now. It is never sent as `working_line`: an app shows nothing of a turn in the bot's own chat;
  - `workingLine` on that member's entry in the group's `working` (`GET /api/group-chats/:id/chat`), beside `openingLine`: what the line reads now;
  - on the turn's run as on any run: `workingLine` in `GET /api/runs/:runId` and `{ "type": "working_line", "text" }` in its stream.
  - **What an app draws for a member at work,** by the turn's `runId`: the newest `text` of its `group_working_line`, or its `workingLine` when the chat is read. Until the turn has one, its opening line (`group_opening_line`, `openingLine`), and "Working" when it has neither. Once a `group_working_line` came for a turn, ignore `group_opening_line` for that turn. `group_opening_line` and `openingLine` are sent as before, so an app that does not know a turn's working line shows the opening line for the whole turn, and an app that knows it shows the opening line against a server that sends a turn no working line. No entry of `features` beside `working-line` tells the two servers apart: the rule above is right against both.
  - **When it goes:** on that turn's `group_run_finished` (same `id` as `runId`), whatever its `status`, and on "Stop all"; no `group_working_line` for a turn comes after it. The next member's line comes with its own turn. "Waiting for your OK" (`group_run_waits`) takes the line's place as in a bot's chat.
- **The Chief of Staff** has no `working_line`: its line is the `activity` of the reply it is writing, `{ tool, text }` (see The Chief of Staff), and its words come from the same place as a bot's. This needs no entry in `features`: draw `text` whenever it is there.
  - `text` is the whole line as it reads now, plain and one line, and it reads as a bot's line does. It starts with the Chief's opening line, when it wrote one: the step that comes with the opening line is what that line announced and does not take its place, the line stays while the Chief thinks after that step, and a step without words leaves it as it was. The first step with words that follows takes its place, and from then on the line is what the Chief is doing: for a tool a bot can also use, the words a bot's line has for that step ("Reading nytimes.com", "Searching the web", "Reading plan.md", "Running a command", "Using the skill meal-plan"); for the Chief's own tools, its own words ("Saving a note", "Reading notes", "Making a group", "Starting a bot", "Checking your bots", "Waiting for Web Surfer", and "Waiting for the bot to finish" for a run it cannot name); and "Thinking" between steps and while a tool without words runs. A reply without an opening line reads the words of its first step. There is no `activity` before the first tool call: show "Thinking".
  - `tool` is the tool that is going on, by its name as before (a Chief tool without its `mcp__botdot__` before it), or `thinking` when none is. It can differ from what `text` says while the opening line stands. An app has no words of its own for it.
  - **At most once a second,** as a bot's line: the server sends a change of the `activity` at most once a second and always sends the last one. The stream and the pending reply's `activity` change together: both give what was sent last. It goes at once when the reply ends, with its answer, a failure or Stop: the pending reply has no `activity` then, and no `activity` comes after the stream's `done`.
  - The Chief's `opening_line` and `openingLine` are sent as before (see The opening line), for an app that reads them. An app that draws the `activity` draws its `text` and nothing else for the line; where an `activity` has no `text`, or none came yet, it shows the chat's opening line when it has one, else "Working" for an `activity` without `text` and "Thinking" for none. That is right against a server from before this too, which sends `text` for "Thinking", for a run the Chief waits for and for its memory and group tools only: there the line reads the opening line until the first of those.

## The reply draft

With `reply-draft` in `features`, the botdot server sends the reply of a bot, the Chief or a member of a group as it is written, and an app draws it in the place of the reply that is being written, the bubble its reply will have, growing there ([ADR 042](adr/042-a-reply-is-sent-as-it-is-written-on-the-streams-that-follow-it.md)). The working line and Stop stay as they are. Against a server without the feature, or in an app that does not know it, the reply comes whole when the run ends, as before.

- **What it is.** `{ "type": "reply_draft", "text" }`: the whole draft so far, never only what is new, so an event can be drawn alone and drawn again. `text` is Markdown as a reply is, never markup: draw it with the reply's own Markdown, by the rules for a draft below. `""` says a draft that was shown is gone: the text was followed by a tool call, and was not the reply. The server reads it from Claude Code's partial messages (`--include-partial-messages`) and keeps it for the running run or turn only: it is not in the run's log or `events`, its record, `result`, the Chief's chat or a notification.
- **Only the reply.** Text a tool call follows is no draft: the draft starts again after each tool call. One line of at most 120 characters is held until it is longer, breaks a line or ends its message, since it may be the opening line (see The opening line): a short reply shows once it is whole. A line of `[[choices: …]]` or `[[ask: …]]` at its end, or the beginning of one, is never in it, and a draft that is whole is the reply as the run's end has it. In a group, a turn that could still be the pass word (see A group's chat, "A pass") sends no draft: a pass shows nothing at all.
- **At most once every 200 ms** for one run or turn. The newest text waits for its turn and is sent then; a draft that waits when the run ends is not sent: the end brings the reply.
- **Where it is sent,** to who follows that run or chat only, never on the feed (`/api/events`):
  - a bot's run and a turn in a group: `GET /api/runs/:runId/stream`, with and without `?simple`. A group's chat follows the run stream of each turn at work (its `runId` from `group_run_started`, `group_working_line` or the chat's `working`) while the chat is open, and closes it when the turn ends or the chat closes;
  - the Chief: `GET /api/chief/chats/:chatId/stream`.
  - A client that connects while the reply is written gets the draft as it was sent last, once, after what already happened was replayed and after the run's lines; then the drafts that follow.
- **When it goes.** On the run stream's `run_end` (`end` with `?simple`) and the Chief stream's `done`, whatever the run's `status`: draw the reply as you would without drafts, in the draft's place. After Stop the chat shows what it shows for a stopped run, with no draft left. In a group a turn's draft may stand until its reply is among the chat's replies, so that no row goes blank between the two; a pass (`passed` on `group_run_finished`) takes it away at once.
- **Drawing a draft.** It is Markdown that is still being written. Before drawing it, both apps (`draftSource` in `public/app.js` and in `Markdown.kt`, the `stream` cases of `test/fixtures/markdown-cases.json`): draw a code block that is not closed yet as code, with a closing fence of their own; leave out a fence that is still being opened or closed; leave out the last line while it is a row of a table (a table shows its whole rows only); and draw a `mermaid` block as plain code, closed or not (no diagram on the web page, no "Open on web" in the Android app, [ADR 041](adr/041-the-web-page-draws-mermaid-diagrams-with-a-vendored-mermaid-in-a-frame-of-its-own.md)): it is drawn as a diagram once the reply has ended. An unclosed `**` or `[` is text until it is closed. Redraw at most once a frame, and keep the chat at its end only when the owner is near it. There is no animation.
- **It is not a reply.** No `unread`, no dot, no "New" line and no notification: the run's end notifies as before, once.

## The plan

With `plan` in `features`, the botdot server keeps the plan of a bot's run and of a Chief reply: the steps of work of several steps, as the bot wrote them with Claude Code's TodoWrite tool ([ADR 057](adr/057-a-plan-is-what-the-bot-writes-with-todowrite.md)). An app draws it above the message box of that chat while the work goes on, folded under the reply once it is done, and as stopped when the work was stopped or failed before its steps were done. Every bot and the Chief are told to make one for work of several steps.

- **What it is.** `{ "steps": [{ "text", "status", "doing"? }] }`: `status` is `"done"`, `"doing"` or `"todo"`, and the step at work may carry `doing`, the words of what it is doing ("Fetching the supplier pages"), when they say more than its `text`. `text` and `doing` are one line of plain text each, at most 200 characters, never markup: draw them as text. A plan has 1 to 50 steps. A run or a reply without a plan has no `plan` field; a plan the bot cleared is gone. Only the TodoWrite tool makes a plan: steps written in a reply are not one, and a helper's own plan (`use helpers`) is not its bot's.
- **Where it is,** for a bot's run:
  - `plan` on the run: in `GET /api/runs`, `GET /api/runs/:runId` and the run's record, while it runs (as it reads now) and after it ended (as it was last written). It is stored with the run, so a reload, a phone that opens the chat later and a restart of the server show the same plan;
  - `{ "type": "plan", "plan" }` in `GET /api/runs/:runId/stream` (with and without `?simple`), each time it changes, with `"plan": null` when the bot cleared it; a client that connects later is given the plan as it reads now, once, after what already happened was replayed. A TodoWrite call is not a `step` of `?simple`, and the plan's changes are not in the run's `events`: its log holds the TodoWrite calls.
- **For the Chief:** `plan` on its reply in the chat's `messages` (`GET /api/chief/chats/:chatId`), on the pending reply while it is written and kept on the reply when it ends; and `{ type: "plan", plan }` on `GET /api/chief/chats/:chatId/stream` as it changes, again for a client that connects later.
- **What an app draws:**
  - **While the work goes on** (a running run, a pending reply with `plan`): a card above the message box, "Plan · 2 of 5 done" with a thin bar of how much is done, the steps done struck through, the step at work marked, with its `doing` under it, and the steps to do. The step it is on is the step at work, else the first that is not done. Folded, it is one line: "Plan · step 3 of 5", the step at work and the bar; a tap opens it, and the app keeps the fold for each chat. A plan of more than 6 steps folds its done steps into one row ("3 steps done · show") and its list has a most height, fading at its end. On Android the card is a dock with a grab handle and a ring for how much is done, folded at first.
  - **Done** (a run that ended with `status: "success"`, a reply without `error`): no card; the reply carries the plan folded, "Plan · 5 of 5 done", that opens to its steps.
  - **Stopped** (the newest run of the chat that ended otherwise, or the chat's last reply with `error`, whose plan has a step that is not done): the card says "Stopped at step 6 of 12": how many steps were done, the step it was on, and how many were not started, with one Dismiss. There is no "Carry on": the owner writes to go on. A dismissed plan is gone from that app (it keeps which ones), and the reply carries it folded as a done one does.
- **It is not a reply.** It never counts as new: no `unread`, no dot, no "New" line and no notification.
- **Without the feature** there is no `plan` field and no `plan` event, and an app shows no plan.

## Search

`GET /api/search?q=…` finds messages in every chat (every word must match; the last may be half typed). Each hit has `at` and `snippet` (matched words in «…»), and either `runId`, `bot` (`{ id, name, color }`) and `fromBot` for a message between bots, or `chatId`, `index` (the message in that Chief chat) and `title`. With `groups` in `features`, `&groups=1` adds the turns in the groups that exist: such a hit has `runId`, `bot` and `group` (`{ id, name }`), and opens in the group. Without it no turn in a group is a hit.

## Memory
