A Chief who asked is told in its chat; a bot that proposed it in a run carries on, told "The owner allowed you to: tell <address> when a run of the job “<job>” ends, signed with the secret in the Vault item “Notify secret: <bot> / <job>”, which no bot is given." Never the secret.
- **Turning it off** is at once, with no card: `DELETE /api/bots/:id/jobs/:jobId/notify/setup` (the Chief's `job_notify_off`, a bot's `job_notify_off` on its own job, or the owner), or `PUT` with `{ notifyUrl: null }`. A proposal that waits is taken back with it. The secret stays in the Vault.
- `POST /api/bots/:id/jobs/:jobId/notify/setup`: a new secret, at once (the Chief's `new_job_notify_secret`; never a bot's). It replaces the value of the same Vault item, and the next notification is signed with it. `409` for a job without an address. `GET` answers as `GET …/call/setup`: `notifyUrl` (the approved address or `null`), `notifyWaiting` (an address that waits for the owner, or `null`), `notifySecretItem` (the Vault item's name, or `null`), `notifyFailed` (the last five failures, newest first, each `{ at, runId, why }`), never the secret.

**What is sent.** When a run of the job ends, however it ends (`success`, `error`, `stopped`, `interrupted`), and when a script run found nothing (`quiet`, with no run kept: `runId` is `null`): one `POST` to the address, after the run and apart from it. A script whose output the AI looks at is told once, when the AI's run ends. The body is JSON:

```json
{ "event": "run.ended", "bot": { "id": "…", "name": "Garage Watch" }, "job": { "id": "…", "name": "Motion" },
  "runId": "…", "status": "success", "text": "A cat crossed the drive.", "at": "2026-10-19T09:41:00.000Z" }
```

`text` is the run's reply, or for a run that failed what went wrong (a script's error and output), cut off at 16 KB. Headers: `Content-Type: application/json`, `User-Agent: botdot`, `X-Botdot-Timestamp` (seconds since 1970, as text), `X-Botdot-Signature-256` and `X-Botdot-Delivery` (an id that is the same on every try of one notification, so a receiver can drop a repeat).

**Checking it**, on the receiver: compute HMAC-SHA256 with the secret (the text from the Vault, as UTF-8 bytes) as the key over `<X-Botdot-Timestamp>.<raw body>` (the header's value, a dot, then the body exactly as received, before any JSON parsing), write it as lowercase hex, and compare `sha256=<hex>` with `X-Botdot-Signature-256` in constant time. Refuse a request whose timestamp is more than five minutes from the receiver's clock, so that one recorded cannot be played again later. In Node.js:

```js
const expected = 'sha256=' + crypto.createHmac('sha256', secret).update(`${req.headers['x-botdot-timestamp']}.${rawBody}`).digest('hex');
const ok = expected.length === sig.length && crypto.timingSafeEqual(Buffer.from(expected), Buffer.from(sig))
  && Math.abs(Date.now() / 1000 - Number(req.headers['x-botdot-timestamp'])) <= 300;
```

**Tries and failures.** Each try waits at most 5 seconds for an answer. A `2xx` answer is a success; what the receiver answers is not read. No answer, a connection that fails, `5xx`, `408` and `429` are tried again, twice, after 1 and then 4 seconds; any other `4xx`, and a redirect (`3xx`, which is never followed), are not. A notification never holds up the run, its report or the job's next run. A notification that failed in the end, or was not sent (a private address, no secret), is logged on the server and kept with the job, the last 20: `notifyFailed` above, and the Chief's checkup is told of those in its window. A test server takes `http://127.0.0.1` as a notify address with `BOT_DOT_NOTIFY_LOCAL=1`, for the tests' own receiver; nothing else sets it.

## The Chief of Staff

- `GET /api/chief`: `{ chief, chats: [{ id, title, updatedAt, … }] }`.
- `POST /api/chief/chats`: a new chat. Returns `{ id, … }`.
- `GET /api/chief/chats/:chatId`: `{ id, title, messages: [{ role: "user" | "chief", text, attachments?, actions?, files?, error?, pending?, unasked?, rating?, checkup?, notice?, startedBy?, read?, relevant?, forgotten?, phases?, at }], busy, queued }` (`startedBy`, `read`, `relevant`: see The Chief's memory; `phases`: see Times, under Bots). `queued`, when `features` has `message-waits`: the owner's messages that wait for the reply to end (see The Chief takes a message while it replies). A Chief message need not follow one of the owner's: botdot starts a Chief turn itself when a bot asked the Chief for a tool (a `report` message with the bot's request comes before the reply), when an install the owner approved has ended, when the owner said yes on a card for something the Chief asked for in that chat (see What follows a yes), and when a bot the Chief started has ended its run after the Chief's reply (see A late result). `error` on a Chief message marks a reply that failed or was stopped (see A failed reply). An entry of `actions` is `{ kind, text, botId?, runId?, groupId?, waits? }`: `text` is the chip ("Created News Watcher", "Updated News Watcher", "Deleted News Watcher"; "Made the group Dev: Coder and Reviewer", see The Chief and groups); see What waits after the Chief's change. A memory change that is done is not among them, only one that waits for the owner (The Chief's memory); the chat keeps it. The same holds for the messages of the answer to `POST /api/chief/chats/:chatId/messages`. A message with `notice: true` is a line botdot wrote itself, with no AI; draw it as a Chief message. A message with `role: "report"` is a bot's own message in the chat (`from`: the bot, `runId`, `error: true` when it didn't finish): a file it sent while it worked on a task the Chief handed it, its request to the Chief for a tool, or its final reply where the Chief's turn about that reply brought none (see A late result; a server without `message-waits` posts it for every such reply, with no turn); show it as that bot's message. `offset` and `total` say where `messages` sit in the whole chat: `?limit=N` gives only its last N messages, `?offset=I` those from message I on (with `limit`, N of them), so a long chat can be read in parts.
- `POST /api/chief/chats/:chatId/messages` with `{ text, attachments?, key? }`: send; `key`: see A message sent twice. Answers `201` with the chat, its `busy` and its `queued`. While a reply is going the message is not refused: it waits its turn (see The Chief takes a message while it replies). The reply streams from:
- `GET /api/chief/chats/:chatId/stream`: SSE `data:` lines with `{ type: "activity", tool, text, input? }` (what it is doing: `text` is the whole line to draw, its opening line first, sent at most once a second, see The working line; a client that connects while the reply is written gets the `activity` as it was sent last, and the pending reply in the chat's `messages` has the same `activity: { tool, text }` until the reply ends), `{ type: "action", action }` (something it changed, like "Created 📰 News"; none for a memory change that is done, see The Chief's memory), `{ type: "opening_line", text }` (its opening line, see The opening line), `{ type: "reply_draft", text }` (its reply as it is written so far, with `reply-draft` in `features`, see The reply draft) and `{ type: "done", … }`, which ends the stream. Then read the chat again for the reply.
- `POST /api/chief/chats/:chatId/stop`: stop the reply that is going, `{ ok: true }`, or `{ ok: false }` when none was (see Stop in a Chief chat). `DELETE /api/chief/chats/:chatId`.
- **Stop in a Chief chat**, when `features` has `ended-by` ([ADR 012](adr/012-a-run-keeps-who-ended-it-and-a-stop-ends-what-its-turn-started.md)). One Stop ends the reply and every bot run that reply started (`run_bot`, `run_job`) and that is still going: each of those runs is `stopped` with `endedBy: "owner"`. A run of that reply that still waits to start is taken away and never starts. A run that had finished, a run an earlier reply started and a teammate's run that a stopped run had asked for are left alone. A job's run that the reply's `run_job` left waiting behind a busy bot is taken away too, and named nowhere: it had no run yet. A script job (one without AI) that the reply's `run_job` started is ended too: the script's processes are killed, as its time limit kills them, and its run is `stopped` with `endedBy: "owner"`, whatever it had printed; the AI does not look at its output. That run is named with the others. When the script was done already, what is left of it is the AI run it handed its output to: one that still waits to start is taken away and named nowhere, and the script's own run then shows in the bot's chat; one that is going is ended and named. The answer of `run_job` for a script is `{ started: "script" }` as before: it carries no run's id.
  - The reply's `error` names what was ended with it: "You stopped this reply." alone, "You stopped this reply. Web Surfer's run was stopped too." for one run, "You stopped this reply. The runs of Web Surfer and Mail Bot were stopped too." for several (each bot is named once). With runs ended, the message has `stopped: [{ botId, name, runId }]`, the runs in the order the reply asked for them, one that still waited to start among them: its `runId` names no run. A server without `ended-by` ends the reply alone.
  - What waits starts: the owner's waiting message in that chat as its own turn (see The Chief takes a message while it replies), and for each bot whose run was stopped what waits for that bot, once its run has ended. The Chief gets no turn for a run that Stop ended.
  - A reply that a sign-out of Claude ended has the `error` "This reply was stopped because Claude was signed out.", one that wrote nothing for 20 minutes "I stopped because nothing happened for 20 minutes: I seemed stuck. Send it again, or break it into smaller pieces.", and one that went on for 4 hours "I stopped after 4 hours: this took too long. Send it again, or break it into smaller pieces." None has `stopped`: the silence and the limit end the turn alone, and a sign-out ends every run by itself. All are failed replies: the `chief_reply` event has `status: "error"`.
- **A failed reply**, when `features` has `needs-text`. A Chief message has `error` whenever its turn ended in an error or was ended (by the owner, a sign-out of Claude or the time limit), also when it has `text`: draw the text, and the reply as failed. A message that succeeded has no `error`.
  - The turn ended in an error: `error` says what went wrong, in words to show. `text` holds only what the Chief wrote before it, or is empty: the text of an error result is never the reply.
  - The owner stopped it: `error` begins with "You stopped this reply.", and `text` is what it had written. With `ended-by` in `features` the sentence goes on with the runs that were stopped too, and a reply that a sign-out or the limit ended has its own sentence (see Stop in a Chief chat).
  - It ended well and wrote nothing: `error` is "I wrote no reply. Ask me what came of it." when the message has `actions`, and otherwise the sentence of a reply botdot could not read.
  - The `chief_reply` event has `status: "error"` whenever the message has `error` and the owner did not stop it, and `"stopped"` when the owner did; its `text` and `error` are the message's. A server without `needs-text` may store the text of an error result as the message's `text`, gives a stopped message `error` only when it has no `text`, and sends `status: "error"` only for a message without `text`.
- **What waits after the Chief's change**, when `features` has `needs-text`. The chip of a bot the Chief created reads "Created <name>", of one it changed "Updated <name>", or only "<name>" when everything of that change waits for the owner, and of one it deleted "Deleted <name>", with the name the bot had. When part of a creation or change was held back for the owner, the action has `waits`: one line in the server's words, "web access waits for your OK" or "web access and commands wait for your OK", from the `waiting` of the bot's answer (see Bots). Show it with the chip as it is; an action without `waits` has nothing waiting.
- A chat can start with a bot's suggestion: a message `{ role: "suggestion", from, text, status: "pending" | "answered" | "dismissed" }`. The Chief does nothing until you answer. Sending `"Approved. Go ahead."` (what the Approve button sends; older apps send `"Build it."`) answers it, and the Chief gets the bot's text with your answer. Any other message is your own, with the plan as background, and the plan keeps waiting. `POST /api/chief/chats/:chatId/dismiss` turns it down.

### The Chief's name

When `features` has `chief-name`, the owner names the Chief of Staff (issue #41). Its name is `chief.name` of `GET /api/chief`, `chiefName` of the feed's `hello` and of `GET /api/setup`, and `"Chief of Staff"` when the owner gave none. An app shows that name wherever it names the Chief: its place in the list, its chat, its settings, a mention, a notification, a card. The server uses it too where it names the Chief to the apps (`botName` of a card the Chief asked for, the `bot` of a memory change it asked for), and tells the Chief its name with its instructions. The role words "the Chief" in an app's own sentences stay.
- **Asked once in the first-run setup:** `GET /api/setup` has `chiefName` and `chiefNameAsked` (whether any app sent an answer). While setup is not `done` and `chiefNameAsked` is `false`, an app asks for the name; `PUT /api/setup` with `{ chiefName }` is the answer, also with `done`: `""` keeps "Chief of Staff", and either way `chiefNameAsked` is `true` from then on. The answer is the setup as it is now, with `chiefName`.
- **Changed later by chat:** there is no control for it (AGENTS.md rule 5). The owner asks the Chief, whose `set_your_name` tool calls `PUT /api/settings/chief-name` with `{ name }` and gets `{ name }`; the Chief may do it only in a turn the owner started (`403` otherwise). The owner's own `PUT /api/settings` with `chiefName` does the same.
- **What is refused:** a name of more than 40 characters, and a name a bot has (compared as a mention writes it, without spaces and case: "news watcher" is News Watcher's): `400` with the sentence to show, and the name stays. Spaces are made plain and the name is kept on one line. `"Chief of Staff"` and `""` are the same: no name.
- Every change is announced with the feed's `chief_name`. A mention of the Chief is suggested with its name (`@Ada`), and `@ChiefOfStaff` still means the Chief beside it, so that habit and older messages keep working; it is not suggested once the Chief has a name (test/fixtures/mentions.json, world `renamed`).
- **A bot can't take it:** `POST /api/bots` and a `PUT /api/bots/:id` that changes `name` are refused (`400`, with the sentence to show) for the Chief's name and for "Chief of Staff", compared as a mention writes them: "Ada is the name of your Chief of Staff. Give the bot another name.", or '"Chief of Staff" names the Chief of Staff. Give the bot another name.'. Nothing of the request is kept. A bot that has such a name already keeps it, and a change that leaves its name as it is goes through.
- A server without `chief-name` sends `"Chief of Staff"` as `chief.name`, no `chiefName`, and an app asks nothing.

### The Chief's skills

When `features` has `chief-skills`, the owner gives library skills to the Chief as to a bot, and its turns load them beside its own `chief:*` skills, as `botdot:<name>` (issue #34).

- **Its list** is `chiefSkills` in `GET /api/settings` (`settings.chiefSkills`: skill names, `[]` to start with). `PUT /api/settings` with `{ chiefSkills: [names] }` replaces it; names that are not in the library are dropped, and the answer has the list as kept. Each change is a change of the Chief's instructions: its next turn in every chat is told them again.
- **On the Skills page**, the Chief is one of the bots by its id `"chief"`: a library skill's `usedBy` (`GET /api/skills`, `GET /api/skills/:name`) has `"chief"` when the Chief has it, and `bots` in `POST /api/skills`, `PUT /api/skills/:name` and `POST /api/skills/import/confirm` takes `"chief"` to give it (with `PUT`, leaving it out takes it away). A renamed skill stays with the Chief; a deleted one leaves it.
- **Only the owner** gives the Chief a skill or takes one away. The Chief's own tools: `"chief"` in their `bots` is ignored and their `bots` never takes a skill from the Chief, a `PUT /api/skills/:name` of a skill the Chief has is refused with `403 { error }`, and `PUT /api/settings` stays the owner's. A bot's `update_skill` of a skill it wrote answers `{ ok: false, error }` once the Chief has that skill too.
- A server without `chief-skills` keeps no list: an app shows no choice for the Chief there.

### The Chief's memory

[ADR 017](adr/017-the-chief-files-a-note-with-a-bot-in-the-owners-turns.md). The Chief has notes of its own (`owner: "chief"`), files a note where it belongs, and lists, corrects, removes, restores and forgets notes wherever they are. No app draws any of it: the owner asks the Chief. An app needs nothing new for it, so `features` announces nothing: the chip of a change that waits is the action's `text`, and the change is a guarded change (Memory, The contract).

**Whose turn.** A Chief message with `role: "chief"` has `startedBy`: `"owner"` when the owner's message started the turn, `"botdot"` when botdot did (a bot's late result, an answered card, a bot's request for a tool, a look's unasked message). A message from before has none. It has `read` once the turn has read something that is not the owner's own word, the kinds in the order they came: `"bot"` (a bot's run or reply, a bot in full, a past run from recall, a note that is not the owner's, a bot's result or plan quoted into the turn), `"web"` (the Board, a registry, a catalogue, a link, a web search or a web page the Chief read itself; a turn in a chat that read the web before starts with it, see The Chief reads the web), `"file"`, `"look"` (a look's unasked message quoted into the turn), `"reminder"` (a reminder that went off, quoted into the turn: a turn botdot started may have set it) and `"unknown"` (a tool the server has no word for). The server marks the turn when it asks for the tool, before the tool answers. `relevant`, on a turn the owner started, lists what the lookup handed it: `[{ kind: "note" | "run", id }]`.

- Only a turn the owner started is given the tools that write memory (`remember`, `update_note`, `remove_note`, `restore_note`, `forget`), and `POST /api/chief/memory` answers `403 { error }` to a call from any other turn: one botdot started, a look, a chat where no reply is going, or no chat at all (`X-Chief-Chat`).
- In such a turn, what it has read decides. Before it read anything (`read` is absent): a note it files has `source: "owner"`, and a forget, or a change to a note of the owner's, is done at once. After: a note it files has `source: "outside"`, a change to a note of the owner's is done at once ([ADR 059](adr/059-a-note-is-saved-without-an-ok.md)), and the forget or a removal of such a note waits as a guarded change, with `by: "chief"`, `botId: null`, `source: "outside"` and, in `GET /api/memory/pending`, `bot` as the Chief (`{ id: "chief", name, emoji, color }`). Notes that are not the owner's are changed and removed at once in both.
- Before a turn the owner started, the Chief is given the lookup (Memory, The contract) over its own notes, the shared notes that are not pinned and its earlier replies, in the turn's message. Its own pinned notes and the pinned shared notes are sent with every turn.

**The routes** are the Chief's alone (`403` for the owner):

- `GET /api/chief/memory?of=<everyone | me | a bot's name or id>`: one place's notes, pinned first, then the newest change first, in pages: `{ of, notes: [{ id, text, topic?, saidBy, day, until?, pinned }], total, offset, limit, next? }`. `saidBy` is the note's `source`; `limit` is 20 unless given, 50 at most; `next` is the offset of the following page. `saidBy=owner` lists only what the owner said. `removed=1` lists the removed notes that can still be brought back, each with `removedOn`, and only in a turn the owner started (else `notes: []` and `say`). `404 { error }` for a name no bot has, or that several have. A page that holds a note which is not the owner's counts as read by the turn.
- `POST /api/chief/memory` with `action`:
  - `"add"`, with `text`, `place` (`"everyone"`, `"me"` or `"bots"`), and for `"bots"` `bots` (names or ids, 30 at most), and optionally `topic` and `until`: `{ ok, result, say, chip?, notes?, saved, already?, notSaved? }`. `result` is `"saved"`, `"already"` (the same note was there in every place: no chip) or `"none"`. `saved` is `[{ id, bot?, saidBy, pinned }]`, `already` `[{ id, bot? }]`, and `notSaved` `[{ bot, why }]`: a bot that does not exist or was deleted, a name several bots have, a bot whose notes are full. The others are saved. A note filed with a bot is pinned in that bot's notes and in its next prompt; no run is started and the bot is sent nothing.
  - `"edit"`, with `id` and any of `text`, `topic`, `until`, `reason`: `result: "saved"`, `"already"` (nothing changed: no chip) or `"waits"`.
  - `"remove"`, with `id` and `reason`: `result: "saved"` or `"waits"`. `"restore"`, with `id`: `"saved"`, with `unpinned` (the ids of the notes that left a full pinned set for it, which `say` and `chip` name) when there are some, or `"already"` when the same sentence was saved again meanwhile.
  - `"forget"`, with `notes` (ids of notes, whoever holds them, removed ones too), `runs` (ids of bots' runs and of the Chief's replies, `chief-<chat>-<n>`), `about` and `reason`: `{ ok, result: "forgotten", say, chip, erased, leftOut, still }`, as a bot's forget, with `still` searched in the shared notes, the Chief's, every bot's and every run. The reply that asks is left out too. `runs` alone leaves those runs out and erases no note. `still.waiting` is searched in every waiting change, whoever asked for it. When `still` holds a note that is not the owner's own word, a run the owner did not start or a waiting change, the turn has read it: a forget that follows waits. `result: "waits"` when the turn has read something. `404 { error }` when nothing named exists.
  - Every answer has `say`, the sentence for the Chief. `chip` is the whole line an app prints, written from what the store answered, and `notes` the ids it is about. `id` is the waiting change of a `"waits"`. `404 { error }` for a note that does not exist.
- The Chief is the author of what it does: a note it files has `by: "chief"`, and an earlier version it leaves has `changedBy: "chief"` and its `reason`.

**The chip** is an action of the reply, `{ kind: "memory", text, notes }`, as the chat keeps it. **An app is sent only the chip of a change that waits** ([ADR 024](adr/024-a-done-memory-change-is-no-row-under-a-reply.md)): one whose `text` begins with `Asked to ` and ends with `(waiting for your OK)`; and the chip of a change to what the owner said after the turn read something, saved at once where it once waited, whose `text` begins with `Changed what you said ` ([ADR 059](adr/059-a-note-is-saved-without-an-ok.md)). Every other `memory` action, a change that is done, is left out of `actions` wherever a Chief message goes to an app (`GET /api/chief/chats/:chatId`, the answer to a message, the `action` and `done` events of the stream), for replies from before this too; the Chief's `say` has it tell the user what such a chip said. The rule needs no new field, so the chat file is as it was, and a reply stored before this is read by it. Print `text` whole. The chip names the place: "Remembered for all bots: …", "Remembered for Coder: …", "Remembered for Coder and Reviewer: …", "Noted for myself: …"; "Updated for Coder: …", "Removed for all bots: …", "Restored for myself: …"; "Forgot 1 note", "Forgot what was said in 2 chats"; and for a change that waits the words of a bot's ("Asked to change what you said: … (waiting for your OK)"). After outside content it says so ("Remembered for Coder, after outside content: …"). A note that was there already, and an edit that changed nothing, make no action. An app from before this prints the same `text`. While one of these tools runs, the `activity` of the stream has the server's `text` ("Saving a note", "Reading notes", "Correcting a note", "Removing a note", "Bringing a note back", "Forgetting").

### The Chief reads the web

[ADR 033](adr/033-the-chief-reads-the-web-and-what-it-changes-after-waits.md). The Chief's turns in a chat have Claude Code's `WebSearch` and `WebFetch`, with no OK for each search. A look and a checkup keep their own tools (ADR 006, ADR 023).

- **A chat that read the web.** Once a turn asks for `WebSearch` or `WebFetch`, its chat's session holds what the web wrote, until the chat starts a new session: that turn and every later turn of the chat have `read` with `"web"` (The Chief's memory).
- **What waits.** In such a chat every call of the Chief's tools that changes something waits for the owner instead of being done: making, changing, running or deleting a bot; a job, its run and its calls; a skill; the Board; a group; a reminder; the memory settings and review; the quiet hours, writing first, the checkup and the Chief's name; removing a tool; making or changing an SSH key. The route answers `202 { held: true, id, say }` and does nothing; the Chief's tool passes that on. Reading is not held, and neither is what asks the owner already or is guarded on its own: `POST /api/asks` (a skill or a tool), `POST /api/vault/requests` (a login), `POST /api/chief/memory` (its notes, as under The Chief's memory), `POST /api/runs/:id/stop` and the checkup's proposals and outcome.
- **The card** is an ask of kind `"chief-action"`, when `features` has `chief-action`. It is listed only for an app that asks with `GET /api/asks?chief-action=1`, which draws its card as below: an app from before it would draw it as a skill to install, with a yes that does it unseen. Its entry in the feed's `asks` is there for every app, with `text` and `yesOn: "card"`, so a notification offers Decline alone. It comes from the Chief (`botId: null`), with `chatId`, `title` (what it does: "Make the bot “Leaker”", "Give Scout a task", "Change the job “Digest” of Scout"), `meta` (why it waits: "The Chief read the web in this chat, so this waits for your OK. A yes does exactly this."), `rows` (what the Chief sent, a `{ label, value }` per field, as an action in a connected app's arguments are, with `more` the same way), `text` ("After reading the web: …") and `yesOn: "card"`. It stands in Needs you and in the Chief's chats. The same request sent again in the same chat while its card waits is the same card. Draw `title`, `meta` and `rows`, with Decline and Approve; a card whose rows are cut short (`more` on a row) or that is too long to show in full (`more` on the ask) offers Decline alone. The same kind of card carries a reset of the account's computer that the Chief asks for (`reset_computer`, see What survives, one reset, and an old computer's files), whether or not its chat read the web: its `meta` then says what the reset erases, and its `text` is "Asks to reset the account's computer".
- **The answer.** `POST /api/asks/:id` with `{ yes: true }` does the request exactly as it was sent, with the Chief's rights (a changed main assignment, rules or a permission of a bot still wait on their own card), and answers `{ ok: true, done, error? }`; `409 { error }` for one too long to show in full. The Chief is told in its chat, by the rules of What follows a yes: a turn of its own, or botdot's line "Approved and done: …" / "Approved, but it failed: …". `{ yes: false }` does nothing, and botdot writes "Declined: …" there.
- **Always allow a type** (#280, [ADR 055](adr/055-always-allow-on-more-cards.md)), when `features` has `more-always`. A card whose request is of a type that may be let through for good has `always` (what its second yes reads: "Always allow reminders", "Always allow changes to your Board", "Always allow changes to your quiet hours", "Always allow changes to when the Chief writes first") and `warning` (what that means, a sentence to ask before it). The types: a new reminder; the Board's layout, a new or changed tile and a tile's value, only when nothing in the request names an address or a saved login (an http read sends a request out; a script still waits on the tile's own card); the quiet hours; writing first. Never a removal, a bot's task, a bot, a job, a skill, an SSH key, the checkup or a card the Chief asks for by name. Draw it between Decline and Approve, not on a card that offers no yes, and ask with `warning` first. `POST /api/asks/:id` with `{ yes: true, always: true }` does the request and lets every later request of that type through in a chat that read the web, answering `always: <type>` beside `done`; `409` on a card without `always`. The feed's entry has `always` and `warning` too. The Chief lists the types with `GET /api/chief/after-web` (`{ types: [{ type, label }] }`, its `after_web_grants`) and removes one with `DELETE /api/chief/after-web` `{ type }` (`remove_after_web_grant`, `404` for one it has not); only a card adds one. Both are free after the web.

### The Chief uses connected apps

[ADR 047](adr/047-the-chief-uses-connected-apps-in-the-owners-turns-and-every-write-waits-for-an-ok.md), when `features` has `chief-connectors`. In a turn the owner started, the Chief finds and runs actions of every connected app with a bot's two tools, `connector_actions` and `connector_run` (lib/connectors-mcp.js), by the rules of An action in a connected app.

- **Who calls.** Such a turn gets a session of its own: a random token botdot makes as the turn starts and forgets as it ends, sent as `X-Run-Token` to `POST /api/connectors/session/actions`, `run` and `wait`, as a bot's run token is. The Chief's key alone opens none of them (`403`). A turn botdot started (a checkup, a look, a reminder, a bot's result, an answered card) gets no session, and neither does a turn while Composio is not set up or no app has a working connection. The one exception is the turn a late yes on the Chief's card starts, below.
- **Which apps.** Every app with a working connection, asked of Composio at each call: an app that is not connected, or was disconnected since the turn started, answers `{ ok: false, error }` with the sentence the Chief passes on, "<App> isn't connected, so nothing was done. Tell the user in one line to connect <App> on the Connectors page; you can't connect it yourself." A file argument (`{"$file": …}`) names only a file the owner attached in that chat.
- **What it did** is a row under the reply, an action `{ kind: "connector", text: "Used Gmail: send email" }`. What an app answers is outside content: the reply's `read` gets `"app"`, and the chat counts as one that read the web (The Chief reads the web), so what the Chief changes in botdot after it waits on a card. The connector routes themselves are not held by that: a write follows its own grants.
- **The card** is an ask of kind `"connector-action"`, as a bot's, with `botId: null`, `chief: true` and `chatId`, the chat it was asked in. Its `text`, `title`, `rows`, `held` and `stoppedAt` are as for a bot; it is the Chief's card (name it with the Chief's name) and stands in Needs you and in the Chief's chats. The Chief's turn waits in the call for ten minutes, as a bot's run does.
- **The answer** is `POST /api/asks/:id`, as for a bot. "Allow once" lets that one call of that turn through. "Always allow this action" is kept for the Chief alone: it lets no bot through, and no bot's grant lets the Chief through. A no is told to the Chief, which says so and does not try another way.
- **A late yes.** Once the wait ended, the card stays with `then: { who: "chief", text: "The Chief does it in “<chat title>” when you allow it.", chatId, here: "The Chief does it in this chat when you allow it." }`. A yes then starts a Chief turn in that chat (or after the reply that is going there), told the action and its stored arguments, with a one-use grant for exactly those; no bot is started. Its answer has `carriesOn: { who: "chief", text: "the Chief does it now" }` or `"the Chief does it when it is free"`. The card goes without an answer when the owner stops that turn, writes in that chat, deletes it, and after 24 hours; "Allow once" on a card whose yes can do nothing any more answers `409`, "<Chief's name> no longer waits for this, so nothing was done. Ask it again.", and the card goes.
- `GET /api/chief/connector-actions` answers `{ actions: ["gmail:GMAIL_SEND_EMAIL", …] }`, the Chief's lasting grants. `DELETE /api/chief/connector-actions` with `{ action }` removes one: `{ ok: true, actions }`, or `404` for one it does not have. The owner and the Chief's tools (`connector_grants`, `remove_connector_grant`) may call both; nothing but a card's yes adds one.

### The Chief takes a message while it replies

When `features` has `message-waits` ([ADR 011](adr/011-a-bot-does-one-thing-at-a-time-and-the-owners-message-waits.md)). A Chief chat has one reply going at a time. A message of the owner to a chat whose reply is going is not refused: it is kept on the botdot server, on disk, with its files, and starts as its own turn when the reply ends. The owner's messages start in the order sent, before the turns botdot starts in that chat. The names are a bot's (see A message that waits); a chat's `waiting` is something else, its plan.

- **A waiting message.** `POST /api/chief/chats/:chatId/messages` to a chat whose reply is going answers `201` with the chat as ever, `busy: true`, and the message in the chat's `queued`. It is not in `messages` until its turn starts. A message that starts at once answers `queued: []`. A quick answer and a plan's "Approved. Go ahead." wait the same way.
- **The list.** `GET /api/chief/chats/:chatId` has `queued`: the owner's messages that wait, oldest first, `[]` when none. An entry is `{ id, text, attachments, at }`, as on a bot: `text` as the owner wrote it (`""` for files alone), `attachments` a list, `at` when it was sent.
- **Too many.** With ten already waiting in that chat: `429 { error: "The Chief already has 10 of your messages waiting here. Take one back, or wait." }`. Nothing is kept and its key is not taken. Keep it in the message box, as a send that failed.
- **The feed.** `queued` with `{ chat: "chief:<chatId>", chatId, queued }`, the whole list, on every change: a message is taken, starts, or is taken back. A message that starts leaves the list just before its turn's `chief_started`.
- **While Claude is signed out** (`hello.claude`, the `claude` event) the owner's waiting messages stay waiting, in `queued` as before, and what botdot has to tell the Chief behind them waits with them: their turn would only fail as not signed in. They start with the sign-in, in their order, and can be taken back meanwhile. A message sent to a chat with no reply going and nothing waiting starts and fails as not signed in, as for a bot.
- **Its turn** is built when it starts, not when the message was sent: a plan that waits in the chat and what the Chief has not seen there are handled then, as for a message sent at that moment. In `messages` it is a `user` message like any other, with `queuedId`, the id it waited under. A file that is gone from the workspace by then is left out. When no turn can be made, the message stands in the chat with a Chief reply that has `error`.
- **Take back.** `DELETE /api/chief/chats/:chatId/queued/:queuedId` answers `200 { text, attachments }`, for the message box, and the message is gone: it starts nothing, and its key may be sent again. Once its turn has started: `409 { error: "It has already started." }`. `404` for an id the chat does not know. Only the owner may call it; the Chief's tools get `403`.
- **After a restart** of botdot the reply that was going is cut off, and what waited starts, once, in the same order. The owner's message starts however long it waited.
- **A deleted chat** takes its waiting messages with it. A chat with a reply going still cannot be deleted.
- **The Chief stops waiting.** While a Chief turn only waits for a bot's run (`wait_for_run`), a message of the owner to that chat ends the wait at once: the open `GET /api/runs/:id/wait?seen=chief` is answered with the run as it is and `ownerWrote: true`, and the tool gives the Chief the run with `note: "The user wrote to you while you waited. Stop waiting: say in one line that <Bot> is still working and that its answer will come to this chat, and end your reply."` The run is not stopped and is not marked as seen by the Chief. A later `wait_for_run` in that turn, while the owner's message still waits, is answered the same at once. A turn that is doing something else is not interrupted: the message waits for the reply to end.
- **For the rules of What follows a yes** a waiting message counts as the owner having written in that chat from when it was sent, also while it waits.
- **Steer**, when `features` has `chief-steer` ([ADR 040](adr/040-a-waiting-message-is-steered-into-the-run-at-its-next-tool-call.md)): as on a bot (see Steer). `POST /api/chief/chats/:chatId/queued/:queuedId/steer` answers `200 { queued }`, that entry with `steering: true`; the reply that is going reads it at its next tool call, and it leaves `queued`. A reply that read one has `steered`, `[{ id, text, attachments, at, readAt }]` in the order read: draw each before that reply, as the owner's message with "Steered" and its time, without buttons. The reply's stream says `{ type: "steered", steered }`, the feed `chief_steered`. Only a reply the owner started takes it; while the Chief answers what botdot brought it (a bot's result, a reminder, an answered card): `409 { error: "<Chief> is answering something botdot brought it, not your message. This starts when it is free." }`, and the message keeps waiting. `409 { error: "It has already started." }` once it was read or started as its own turn; Take back the same. Not read before the reply ends (no more tool calls, Stop, a restart): it loses `steering` and starts as its own turn, once, first. Only the owner may call it; the Chief's tools get `403`. A server without `chief-steer` shows no button for a Chief chat (`404`).
- A server without `message-waits` refuses a message to a chat whose reply is going: `400`, with the reason in `error`.

### A late result

When `features` has `message-waits`. When a run the Chief started ends after the Chief's reply (the Chief stopped waiting for it, or the bot waited for its teammates' answers), the Chief gets one turn in the chat that started it: `chief_started`, then `chief_reply`. botdot tells it how the run ended (finished, failed with its problem, stopped) and what the bot replied, as the bot's text and not the owner's, and the Chief says the result in a few lines.

- **That reply** is a Chief message like any other. Its `actions` begin with `{ kind: "from", text: "From <Bot>", botId, runId }`: draw it as the chip. It carries the run's `files`, as a bot's `report` message does (see Files from bots). No `report` message is added, and the `chief_reply` event has no `from`.
- **No turn** when the owner or the Chief stopped the run (`status: "stopped"`; a run that a sign-out or the limit ended failed, see Who ended it), and none when the Chief had read how it ended, also when it reads it while the turn still waits behind a reply.
- **Behind a reply** that is going in that chat the turn waits, behind the owner's waiting messages, also across a restart.
- **A run that a sign-out of Claude ended** brings no turn, since the Chief can take none: the chat gets the bot's own `report` message at once, with `error: true` and the run's sentence as its `text`.
- **When the turn fails** or cannot start, the chat gets the bot's own `report` message, `{ role: "report", from, text, runId, files?, error? }`, after the failed reply, as a server without `message-waits` adds it in place of the turn. The Chief reads it with the owner's next message.
- **A chat that is gone:** the turn goes to the chat the owner last used, or to a new one.

### A message sent twice

When `features` has `message-key`. An app cannot tell a request that never arrived from one whose answer was lost, so it gives every message the owner sends a key, and botdot takes a key once.

- `POST /api/chief/chats/:chatId/messages` and `POST /api/bots/:id/run` accept `key`: a string of 8 to 64 characters of `A-Z`, `a-z`, `0-9`, `_` and `-`, made by the app. A new one for each message the owner sends, and the same one when that message is sent again. Any other `key` is refused: `400`, with the reason in `error`.
- The first request with a key is handled as one without. A later request with the same key for the same chat or the same bot, within 24 hours, starts nothing and adds nothing, whatever else it holds: it answers `200` with `again: true` in what such a request answers, the chat as it is now, or the run the first request started as it is now (the waiting run, while that message waits its turn: see A message that waits). Treat it as a message that went. It is answered so also while the Chief is still replying to that message, and while the message waits for a reply to end: it waits once.
- A key is kept once its message is taken: saved in the chat, its run started, or kept to wait its turn for a busy bot or a replying Chief. A message the owner takes back gives its key back. A request that was refused does not use its key up: a message refused because ten already wait is sent again with its key, and is then taken.
- A key belongs to its chat or its bot: the same key somewhere else is another message. The keys survive a restart of botdot.
- A request without `key` is handled as before: sent twice, it is taken twice. A server without `message-key` ignores `key`, so an app may always send it; only against a server with `message-key` is sending again after a lost answer safe.

### The Chief writes first

A few times a day the botdot server gives the Chief a moment to look around (a look, [ADR 006](adr/006-a-look-is-its-own-read-only-chief-session.md)), and the Chief may write the owner one message they did not ask for: at most three a day, none in quiet hours, often none at all.

- **An unasked message** is a message `{ role: "chief", text, at, unasked: true, lookId }` at the end of the Chief chat the owner last used, or of a new chat when there is none. `lookId` is botdot's own reference to the look's record of it; apps ignore it. It is a Chief reply like any other: it counts as a new reply, `chief_reply` announces it (with `unasked: true`), and an app that doesn't know `unasked` shows a plain reply. A look that ends with nothing leaves no trace.
- **Rating it**, when `features` has `ratings`: `PUT /api/chief/chats/:chatId/messages/:index/rating` with `{ "rating": "up" | "down" | null }`, where `index` is the message's place in the chat's whole `messages` (as `firstUnread` counts). It answers `{ ok: true, chatId, index, rating }`; the message then has `rating: "up" | "down"`, or no `rating` after `null`, and every app hears `rating` on the feed. Only an unasked message can be rated: any other message, an index outside the chat or another value is refused with `400` and changes nothing. Owner only. Show 👍 and 👎 under unasked messages only, and only with the feature.
- **How often** is the owner's to say to the Chief, not a control. The Chief's tool uses `GET /api/settings/writing-first`, which answers `{ writingFirst: { perDay, sentToday } }`, and `PUT` with `{ "perDay": 0 | 1 | 2 | 3 }` (0 = the Chief never writes first; 3 is how it starts); anything else is refused with `400`.
- **For a test environment:** a botdot server started with `BOT_DOT_LOOK_ON_DEMAND=1` has `POST /api/chief/look`, which starts a look now and answers when it is over: `{ looked: true, posted: true, chatId }`, `{ looked: true, posted: false }` (nothing, or `failed: true`), or `{ looked: false, reason: "off" | "limit" | "quiet-hours" | "busy" | "looking" }`. Owner only; without the variable it answers `404`.

### The Chief's checkup

With `checkup` in `features`. On the days and at the time the owner set, at most once a day, the botdot server gives the Chief a checkup ([ADR 023](adr/023-a-checkup-reads-the-bots-and-asks-none.md)): the Chief reads what botdot recorded since the last one (the chats the owner started with each bot, in groups and with the Chief, failed and stopped runs, each job, messages between bots left unread, the Board's tiles) and proposes at most three changes, or nothing. No bot is asked and none runs. It is not a look: it has its own switch and does not count toward the unasked messages of a day.

- **Its message** opens a new Chief chat titled for the day ("Checkup, 12 Oct"): `{ role: "chief", text, at, checkup: true, choices, startedBy: "botdot" }`. The server writes `text` from the proposals it kept (numbered, each with what kind it is, the bot it is for and the day and words that show why) and `choices` are its answer buttons ("Do 1", "Do all", "Not now"; "Do it" and "Not now" for one proposal). It is a Chief reply like any other: it counts as a new reply and `chief_reply` announces it (with `checkup: true`). It is no unasked message: it has no `unasked` and cannot be rated, so show no 👍 and 👎 under it. An app that doesn't know `checkup` shows a plain reply with buttons. A checkup with nothing to propose opens no chat and announces nothing.
- **The owner's answer** in that chat is an ordinary message and starts an ordinary Chief turn, which makes the changes they picked. Nothing else is needed from an app.
- **The setting:** `GET /api/settings/checkup` answers `{ checkup: { on, days, time, webSearch, when, timeZone, lastRanAt } }`. `on`: whether checkups run (`true` is how it starts). `days`: the days of the week, `0` (Sunday) to `6` (Saturday); `[1, 2, 3, 4, 5]` is how it starts. `time`: `"HH:MM"` in `timeZone`, the time zone of the quiet hours; `"08:00"` is how it starts. `webSearch`: whether a checkup may search the web (`false` is how it starts; search only, it never opens a page it picks). `when`: the days and the time in words, written by the server ("Weekdays at 08:00", "Mondays and Thursdays at 07:30"). `lastRanAt`: when the last checkup ran, or `null`. `inQuietHours: true` is there only when the quiet hours were moved over `time` after it was set: no checkup starts until one of them changes.
- **Changing it:** `PUT /api/settings/checkup` with any of `{ on, days, time, webSearch }` changes what is named and answers the setting as `GET` does; every app hears `checkup` on the feed. `days` also takes English names ("Monday"). Refused with `400` and `{ error }`, changing nothing: a value of the wrong kind, no day, a `time` that is not `HH:MM`, and a `time` inside the quiet hours, also when `on: true` would switch on a checkup whose time is inside them (the error names the nearest time that works). The owner may always call it. The Chief's call is refused with `403` outside a turn the owner started by writing to it, and so is `webSearch: true` once that turn has read anything that is not the owner's (a bot's reply, a page): the owner then uses the switch. `PUT /api/settings` ignores a `checkup` in its body.
- **In the apps:** in the Chief's settings, a "Checkup" switch for `on` with one line under it, `when` followed by ". Ask the Chief to change it.", and a "Web search" switch for `webSearch` under that, shown but not usable while `on` is `false`. The days and the time have no control: the owner tells the Chief. Without `checkup` in `features`, show neither.
- **When the owner asks the Chief for one** in a chat, the Chief's tools use `GET /api/chief/checkup` (`{ since, records, proposedBefore, … the setting }`: the same records as text), `POST /api/chief/checkup/proposals` with `{ proposals: [{ kind: "rule" | "skill" | "job" | "bot" | "tile", bot, change, day, evidence, again? }] }` (answers `{ proposals: [{ n, kind, bot, change, evidence, day }], title, text, choices }` with those it kept, numbered; `{ proposals: [], text: "" }` when none held up) and `POST /api/chief/checkup/outcome` with `{ picked: [n], declined: [n] }` (answers `{ decided: [{ n, status }] }`). The last two need the chat the call comes from (`X-Chief-Chat`) and answer `400` without it, and `403` when no turn the owner started is going there. Such a checkup opens no chat and does not move the scheduled one. Not for apps.
- **What is kept:** a proposal is validated by the server (at most three; a kind it knows; a bot that exists, "Chief of Staff", or a new bot's name; a change; evidence with its day, not after today; the change and the evidence as plain text, with links, addresses and markup taken out) and kept with how the owner answered: one they picked is never proposed again, one they declined comes back only with evidence of a later day than the decline, and one they did not answer may be shown once more. The same proposal is known by the id the Chief names, or by its kind, its bot and its words.
- **For a test environment:** a botdot server started with `BOT_DOT_LOOK_ON_DEMAND=1` has `POST /api/chief/checkup/now`, which starts a checkup now, whatever its switch and its time, and answers when it is over: `{ ran: true, posted: true, chatId }`, `{ ran: true, posted: false }` (nothing, or `failed: true`), or `{ ran: false, reason: "quiet-hours" | "busy" | "running" }`. Without the variable it answers `404`.

## The Board

The owner's page of tiles. Its paths (`/api/dashboard…`), its feed event (`dashboard`) and its data file (`data/dashboard.json`) keep its earlier name, `dashboard`.

Tiles the Chief puts together: `switch`, `button`, `value`, `list` and `note`, grouped under headings.
- `GET /api/dashboard`: `{ sections: [names in order], tiles: [...], pending: [...], suggestions: [...] }`. Tiles without a `section` come first, without a heading. A tile is `{ id, kind, title, section, parent, wide, color, bot, unit, every, approved, tappable, asks, value, at, error }`. `every` is how often it reads again while the Board is open, in seconds, as the owner chose it (10 to 86400, 60 by default), or `0` for only when tapped: it is read once, then again only by a tap that reads it (`POST …/tap`) or after one of its actions. A tile that reads nothing has no `every`; an app shows it in the tile's pop-up ("Refreshes every 15 minutes while the Board is open."), and the Chief changes it (`update_tile`). `value` is `true` / `false` / `null` for a switch, `{ text, level: "ok" | "warn" | "bad" | null }` for a value, a list of lines for a list, and Markdown text for a note. A `group` tile holds the tiles whose `parent` is its id: draw only tiles without a `parent` in the sections, one dot per tile inside on the group, and its tiles below it when it's tapped open. `color` is null when it's the tile's bot's colour. `pending` has the tiles whose script waits for your OK, in full (with their `read`, `do`, `turnOn` and `turnOff` sources, and the scripts in them). With `needs-text` in `features`, each also has `scripts`: every script of the tile as a string, those of its named reads and named actions too, in the order a suggested tile's `scripts` has them, so the approval shows all of them without reading the sources. With `staged-changes`, an approved tile whose scripts someone changed is in `tiles`, approved and working, and in `pending`, both with `change` (see A change that waits). With `proposal-carries-on`, a suggestion a bot made in a run can have `then` (see What follows a yes).
- Every tile also has `size` (`small`, `wide`, `big`: two across and two high, `row`: the whole width) and `look`: what to draw, already filled in, so clients draw it as it comes. `look` is `{ dot, face, panel, tap, short }`:
  - `dot`: `{ filled: true | false | null (unknown), color?, big? }` or null (none). `face` and `panel`: lists of blocks (below); `panel` is null when a tap opens nothing.
  - `tap`: `toggle` (a switch: `POST …/tap` with `on`), `act` (`POST …/tap`), `refresh` (`POST …/tap`), `panel` (open the pop-up with `panel`, titled with the tile's title), `chat` (its bot's chat), `group` (open its tiles) or `none`.
  - `short`: a few words for the one-line strip ("21.5 °C"), or "".
  - A colour (`color`) is `#rrggbb`, or one of `ok`, `warn`, `bad`, `muted`, `faint`, `text` (the theme's); missing means the tile's colour (its `color`, or its bot's).
  - Blocks: `text { text, size?: small|normal|large, muted?, markdown?, lines? }`, `value { text, unit?, color? }`, `dot { filled, color?, label?, big? }`, `list { items, max }` (show `max` of them), `progress` and `gauge { ratio: 0..1 | null, text, color? }`, `chart { points: [numbers], color?, text? }`, `image { src, height?: small|normal|large, fit?: cover|contain }` (`src` is an API path: GET it, as for `/api/files`), `chat { label? }` (a link to the tile's bot), `row` and `column { blocks }`. An empty `text`, `value` or `list` shows a faint dot.
  - Controls have a `key`: `switch { key, on: true|false|null, label? }`, `button { key, label }`, `slider { key, value | null, min, max, step, unit?, percent?, label? }` (send when let go; `percent`: show the value as a percentage of min–max), `color { key, value: "#rrggbb" | null, label? }` (a colour wheel for hue and saturation; on a face, a swatch that opens it), `choice { key, options: [{ label, value }], value, label? }`. Using one: `POST /api/dashboard/tiles/:id/act` with `{ block: key, value }` (a switch: true/false; a button: nothing). Returns the tile, already showing the new value; the reads after it (about 1 and 5 seconds later) come on the feed.
- `GET /api/dashboard/tiles/:id/image/:name`: the cached picture, or 404. It never contacts the login host. `POST` to the same path with `X-Bot-Dot: 1` fetches it.
- While the Board is on screen, call `POST /api/dashboard/refresh` with `X-Bot-Dot: 1` every 15 seconds or so. It refreshes stale values and pictures and returns the Board; changes also come on the feed (`dashboard`). Both `GET /api/dashboard` and `?quiet` only read cached state. Text in `look` can change without a new value: a time shown as words (`{{value|ago}}`, "9 hours ago") is worked out again on every answer, so draw `look` as each answer gives it.
- `POST /api/dashboard/tiles/:id/tap` with `{ on?: true | false }` (a switch: what it should become). A value tile refreshes. Returns the tile. `asks: true` means a tap starts a run of its bot, which shows in its chat: the answer then has `runId`, and with `message-waits` in `features` also `queued: true` when the bot was busy and the request waits its turn (see A message that waits). A tap on a list or note is for the client: show its first 3 lines, and all of it in a pop-up when tapped, with a link to its bot's chat.
- `POST /api/dashboard/tiles/:id/move` with `{ section, parent, before }`: the owner dragged it, under the heading `section` (or into the group `parent`), before the tile `before` (null: at the end there). A group's tiles move along to its new heading.
- `DELETE /api/dashboard/tiles/:id`: remove it (a long press). A group's tiles stay, loose; `?all` removes them too.
- `POST /api/dashboard/tiles/:id/approve` with `{ approve: true | false }`: a tile's script. Declining removes a tile that was never approved (see Decline), and keeps an approved one (see A change that waits). With `ask-continues` in `features`, a tile of `pending` can have `then`, and the answer of a yes `carriesOn` (see What follows a yes).
- `suggestions` (in `GET /api/dashboard`): tiles bots suggested, `[{ id, bot, reason, at, tile: { kind, title, section, wide, scripts, … } }]`. Show them faded, and in that bot's chat. `POST /api/dashboard/suggestions/:id` with `{ add: true | false }`: Add puts it on the Board (returns `{ tile }`); ✕ drops it, and that bot won't suggest it again.

## Attaching files

`POST /api/uploads?to=bot:<botId>` (or `?to=chief`, or `?to=group:<id>` for a message in a group: see The owner's files in a group). The body is the file itself (`Content-Type: application/octet-stream`), with its name in the header `X-File-Name` (URL-encoded). Up to 25 MB.

Returns `{ name, file, size, readable }`. Send that object, as it is, in `attachments` with the message, up to 10 per message. `readable` is set when botdot saved a readable copy (Excel, Word, PowerPoint, OpenDocument).

## Files from bots

A bot can give the owner files with its reply (a PDF, a Word or Excel file…). The run lists them in `files: [{ name, file, size }]`, and a Chief chat's `report` message (a bot's reply passed on there) has them too, as has the Chief's reply about a bot's result (see A late result). The `run_finished` and `chief_reply` events carry `files` as well.

`GET /api/files/<file>` (that is, `/api/files/<runId>/<name>`, each part URL-encoded) downloads one. PDFs come `inline`, everything else as an attachment. They are kept for 90 days; after that it's a 404.

## The document viewer

When `features` has `document-viewer` ([#251](https://github.com/Kordal/bot-dot/issues/251)), the apps open a file in their own viewer: a file chip of a reply, a file you attached, a file in a bot's settings, and a file path a bot wrote in its reply. Without the feature an app downloads the file as before, and a path in a reply stays text.

- **A file as JSON.** `GET /api/files/<runId>/<name>?view=1` and `GET /api/bots/:id/files/<file>?view=1` answer `{ name, size, at, type, kind, truncated, text? }` instead of the file: `type` its media type, `kind` one of `markdown`, `csv`, `image`, `text`, `none` (no preview: a PDF, an Office file, a binary). `text` is there for `markdown`, `csv` and `text`: the file up to 1 MB and 1,000 lines, with `truncated: true` when it was cut. An image is shown from the same address without `?view` (the raw file). `?stat=1` answers the same without `text`, for a hover. Without either, the route downloads the file as before, with the same headers. A file that is gone is `404` with `{ error, state: "gone" }`.
- **A path a bot wrote.** `GET /api/bots/:id/path?path=<path>` (the owner only; the Chief gets `403`) with `&view=1` or `&stat=1` as above, or neither for the raw file. A relative path is in the bot's workspace; `~/…`, `/home/box/…` and `/workspace/…` are on the bot's computer (`computer: "linux"`). Anything else, a `..`, or a link that leads out of those folders is `403` `{ state: "refused" }`; a missing file `404` `{ state: "gone" }`; a folder `400` `{ state: "not-file" }`. A computer that is not running answers `409` `{ state: "computer-off" }` and is not started: the app offers "Start the computer" (`POST /api/bots/:id/computer` with `{ action: "start" }`) and asks again. A file on the computer is read up to 1 MB: above that its view has `downloadable: false` (its text cut as above, a picture with `kind: "none"`) and its raw download is `413`. A path's raw file is sent with `Cache-Control: no-store`, as a bot may rewrite it.
- **A file the owner sent in a Chief chat or a group**, when `features` has `chat-attachments` (#268): `GET /api/chief/chats/:id/uploads/<name>` and `GET /api/group-chats/:id/uploads/<name>`, `<name>` being the last part of the attachment's `file` (`"uploads/<name>"`), URL-encoded, with `?view=1` and `?stat=1` as above, or neither to download it under the name it was sent with (a PDF `inline`, anything else an attachment in a sandbox). Only a file a message of that chat lists, sent, steered into a reply or still waiting, from the Chief's folder or the group's own; anything else is `404` `{ state: "gone" }`, and a link out of that folder `403` `{ state: "refused" }`. Sent with `Cache-Control: no-store`. The owner only: the Chief gets `403`. The apps draw every chip under the owner's message as a link to it (one still uploading, or that failed, stays a plain chip); a file the owner sent in a bot's chat is read with `GET /api/bots/:id/path?path=<file>`.
- **Which text is a path**: a word ending in a file extension the apps know, relative to the bot's workspace or starting with `~/`, `/home/box/` or `/workspace/`, with no `..` (`notes/plan.md`, `~/out/report.csv`, `/workspace/a.txt`, `README.md`; a single capitalised word such as `Node.js` stays text), outside a web address and outside code; an inline `code` span that is exactly one path counts. The rules and the cases both apps are tested on are in `test/fixtures/file-paths.json`.

## A bot's workspace

When `features` has `workspace-files` ([#296](https://github.com/Kordal/bot-dot/issues/296)), a bot's settings have a **Workspace** section next to Files: every file in its folder, not only what it was given. Without the feature the apps show only the Files section. Every route below is the owner's alone: the Chief gets `403`. A bot's own tools are unchanged.

A bot has two folders, each a **root**, named by `?root=`:

- `server` (the default): its workspace on the botdot server (`data/workspaces/<bot>`), where its runs work. Listed, opened, downloaded, uploaded into, and its entries renamed, moved and deleted.
- `computer`, for a bot with `computer: "linux"`: its folder on the account's computer, `/workspace/<bot>`, read there as `box`. Listed, opened, downloaded and deleted in, nothing else (`405`). Only while the computer runs: else `409` `{ state: "computer-off" }`, and nothing starts it; the app offers "Start the computer" (`POST /api/bots/:id/computer` with `{ action: "start" }`) and asks again. A bot without the computer gets `404` `{ state: "no-computer" }`.

`path` is relative to its root, `""` for the top, names joined by `/`. A `..`, a `.`, an absolute path (`/…`, `~…`) or a backslash is `403` `{ state: "refused" }`. On the server botdot follows no link: a link is listed with `type: "link"` and is never opened, listed into, renamed, moved or deleted (`403`), and nothing goes into a folder through one. On the computer a link is listed the same way; a path through a link is refused, a file is opened only when it stays inside the folder once resolved, and deleting a link removes the link, not what it points to. Something that is not there is `404` `{ state: "gone" }`.

- `GET /api/bots/:id/workspace?root=&path=` lists a folder: `{ root, path, entries, more, readOnly }`. An entry is `{ name, path, type, size?, at, protected?, keepsName? }`: `type` is `folder`, `file`, `link` or `other`, `size` (bytes) only for a file, `at` when it last changed. Folders come first, then the rest, each by name; the apps sort by date when asked and fold names starting with `.` under "Show hidden". At most 1,000 entries: `more` says how many more the folder holds ("…and N more"). A file is `400` `{ state: "not-folder" }`.
- `GET /api/bots/:id/workspace/file?root=&path=` downloads a file (`Cache-Control: no-store`; a PDF `inline`, anything else an attachment in a sandbox), and with `&view=1` or `&stat=1` answers as [the document viewer](#the-document-viewer) does. A folder is `400` `{ state: "not-file" }`. On the computer a file comes out up to 1 MB, as a path in a reply.
- `GET /api/bots/:id/workspace/count?root=&path=` says what a delete takes, for its confirmation: `{ type: "file" | "folder", files, folders, capped }`, counted down the folder up to 10,000 (`capped: true` past that). The apps always ask before a delete and name what goes: "Delete the folder notes? It holds 12 files and 2 folders, which go with it."
- `DELETE /api/bots/:id/workspace?root=&path=` deletes a file, or a folder with what it holds. `{ ok: true, path }`. The top itself is `403`.
- `POST /api/bots/:id/workspace/upload?path=<folder>` (server only) takes one file as the raw body, its name URL-encoded in `X-File-Name`, into that folder: `201` `{ name, path, size }`. It never writes over a file: a name that is taken becomes `name (2).ext`. Up to 25 MB (`413`); the apps send at most 10 files at a time.
- `POST /api/bots/:id/workspace/folder` (server only) with `{ path, name }` makes a folder in `path`: `201` `{ ok: true, path }`. A name with `/` in it is `400`; one that is taken `409` `{ state: "exists" }`.
- `POST /api/bots/:id/workspace/move` (server only) with `{ from, to }` renames or moves: `to` is the new path in the same root, its folder already there. `{ ok: true, path }`. Something already at `to` is `409` `{ state: "exists" }`; a folder into itself `400`.

**What botdot keeps** in the workspace is read-only (docs/DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-22): `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/`, `.git/`, `.jobs/` and `.tiles/` at its top, with all they hold, are listed and opened but never deleted, renamed or moved, and nothing is uploaded, made or moved into them (`403` `{ state: "protected" }`); a listing inside one has `readOnly: true`. Such top entries carry `protected: true`. The `uploads/` folder (the [Files section](#bots)) stays itself (`protected: true`), and a file the owner gave the bot keeps its name and place there (`keepsName: true`: deleted here, with its readable copy and its pin, as in Files, but never renamed or moved, `403`).

Every write is in the audit log, `workspace-file.uploaded`, `workspace-file.folder-made`, `workspace-file.moved` (with `to`) and `workspace-file.deleted`, each with the bot, the root and the path, never a file's content.

## The Vault's items

What the owner saved for the bots to use without ever seeing it: in botdot's own vault or, once Bitwarden is connected, in the **bot-dot** folder of the owner's Bitwarden. Only the owner writes here; the Chief's tools read the list and, with `chief-ssh-key-add`, make a new SSH key, with `chief-ssh-key-change` change one's bots and name (below), and with `chief-vault-remove` ask the owner on a card to remove an entry (Things that need the owner), and nothing else. No answer holds a secret.

- `GET /api/vault`: the items, each with `id`, `name`, `kind` and `allowedBots` (the ids of the bots that may use it). `kind` is `login`, `card` or `identity`, with `vault-ssh-keys` also `ssh-key`, and with `vault-files` also `file`; cards, identities, SSH keys and files come from Bitwarden only. Read an item without `kind` as a login: a server from before `vault-types` sends none for the items of its own vault. With Bitwarden locked the list is empty.
  - A `login` also has `type` (below), `site` (the host it is for), `username`, `hasPassword`, `insecureTls` (a self-signed certificate is accepted when bots call its API) and `allowedHosts` (the additional exact hosts it may be used on). From Bitwarden it also has `hasTotp`, and `reprompt` when it asks for the master password again there, so bots can't use it.
  - A `card` has `brand`, `last4`, `expiry` and `holder`; an `identity` has `holder` and `email`. Neither has a `type`.
  - An `ssh-key`, when `features` has `vault-ssh-keys`, is an SSH key item of the bot-dot folder (Bitwarden's own item type). It has `type: "ssh-key"`, `fingerprint` and `publicKey`, and never its private key. Draw it as "SSH key" with its fingerprint, and let the owner choose its bots as for a card (`PUT /api/vault/:id` with `allowedBots`); its key itself is changed in Bitwarden. A bot it is shared with runs commands with it on its own computer (`POST /api/vault/session/computer/run-with-ssh-key`, under [Not for clients](#not-for-clients)); the Chief's `list_vault` shows it with its type, its public key and its fingerprint. An app that does not know the kind may draw it as it draws an unknown kind.
  - A `file`, when `features` has `vault-files`, is a file the owner added (below), kept in the bot-dot folder as a secure note botdot made. It has `type: "file"`, `fileName` (its name, of letters, digits, `.`, `-` and `_`) and `size` (in bytes), and never its content. Draw it as "File" with its file name and size, and let the owner choose its bots as for a card (`PUT /api/vault/:id` with `allowedBots`), saying that botdot puts it on the account's computer for the length of one command, where every bot there could read it meanwhile. A bot it is shared with runs one command with it (`POST /api/vault/session/computer/run-with-file`, under [Not for clients](#not-for-clients)); the Chief's `list_vault` shows it with its type and file name. Any other secure note is not listed.
- **`placedIn`**, when `features` has `vault-file` (#253): where botdot keeps the item's secret in a file, by the owner's yes on a card (see Secrets a bot asks to keep in a file), only when there is one: a list of `{ id, where, botId, at, written }`, `where` in the owner's words ("the account's computer:/etc/traefik/ovh.env", or "admin@nas.example.ts.net:/srv/x.env"), `botId` the bot it is for, `at` when it was last written (or allowed), and `written: false` for a place allowed and not written yet. Draw each with "Remove the file": `DELETE /api/vault/secret-files/:id` deletes it where botdot wrote it (as root on the account's computer, or over SSH with the same saved SSH key) and drops the owner's yes for it, so the bot must ask again. It answers `{ ok: true }`, or `{ ok: true, byHand: true, say }` when its bot is gone or the SSH key is no longer shared with it: the yes is dropped, and `say` tells the owner to delete the file by hand. `404` for one no longer listed, `409` with Bitwarden locked, `502` when it could not be removed (a sentence to show). Only the owner: the Chief's tools get `403`.
- **`type`**, when `features` has `vault-types`: what an item of kind `login` is, one of `login`, `api-token`, `other` and, with `vault-github-app`, `github-app`. Its `kind` stays `login` for all of them. The type is a label and decides which fields the form asks for. It forbids nothing but for `github-app`: the other types are used in an API call and typed in a browser alike. An item saved before the types is a `login`, and nothing is guessed from its name. Draw an item without a `type`, or with one you don't know, as a login.
  - `login`: a username or email in `username`, and a password.
  - `api-token`: a token, and its token ID in `username` where the service has one; the token ID is optional.
  - `other`: a secret alone. Send no `username` with it: a new item then has none, and a saved one keeps the one it has, which the form does not show and shows again when the type is changed back. Its secret may have several lines, e.g. a private key: take it in a masked box of several lines, pasted or read from a file the owner picks (64 KB at most), and send it as it is, line breaks too; the server keeps it byte for byte (#155).
  - `github-app`, when `features` has `vault-github-app` (ADR 037): a GitHub App. `username` is its App ID (a number), with `/` and the installation ID after it when the app is installed on several accounts (`5237335/81`); the secret is its private key (the `.pem` GitHub gave), taken as Other's is. A new one's address starts as `api.github.com` (GitHub Enterprise Server: its own address). A save is `400` with a sentence when the App ID is not a number or the secret is not an RSA private key. A bot never gets the key: wherever a login's secret goes it gets an installation token botdot makes from it, which expires within the hour (`api_request` and Board tiles: `{{token}}` and `{{password}}`; `run_with_login`: `BOTDOT_PASSWORD`, with `x-access-token` in `BOTDOT_USERNAME`), and typing it into a browser is refused. Draw it as "GitHub App" with 🐙, its App ID and "key saved". To a server without `vault-github-app`, offer no GitHub App; draw an item of that type you meet as its type says if you know it.
- **`inComputer`**, when `features` has `vault-in-computer`: "Usable in its computer". A `login` has it, `false` unless the owner turned it on. With it on, a bot the login is shared with may run a command on the account's computer, which every bot with a computer works on, with the login in that command's environment (`POST /api/vault/session/computer/run-with-login`, under [Not for clients](#not-for-clients)); off, it is refused. It is off by default and only the owner changes it, in the Vault's editor or with Allow on a bot's card that asks for it (see A switch of a saved login, with `vault-switch`): draw it there as a switch labelled "Usable in its computer", under the certificate's, and say what it means: every bot on the account's computer can read the secret then (they all work there with sudo), so it is for secrets that guard something inside that computer, such as the local Proton Bridge password. A login with it on shows "usable in its computer" at the end of its line in the list. To a server without `vault-in-computer`, send no `inComputer` and show no switch. With Bitwarden it is botdot's own, kept beside the item's bots and not in Bitwarden.
- `POST /api/vault` with `{ name?, site, username?, password, type?, allowedBots?, allowedHosts?, insecureTls?, inComputer? }`: a new item of kind `login`; `201` with the item. `site` and `password` are needed. Without `type` (left out, or `null`) it is a `login`.
- `PUT /api/vault/:id` with any of those: what is sent changes, and an empty `password` keeps the secret. Without `type` (left out, or `null`) the item keeps its type, and a `type` alone changes nothing else of the item: its address, username, secret, bots and additional hosts stay. With Bitwarden the type is botdot's own, kept beside the item's bots and not in Bitwarden, and the Bitwarden item is edited only when its name, address, username or secret really change. For a card or an identity send `allowedBots`; a `type` sent for one is not kept. `404` for an unknown id.
- `POST /api/vault/ssh-keys` with `{ name, privateKey }` or `{ name, generate: true }`, and `allowedBots?`, when `features` has `vault-ssh-key-add`: a new SSH key in the bot-dot folder, as Bitwarden's own SSH key item; `201` with the item as the list shows it (`kind: "ssh-key"`, `fingerprint`, `publicKey`). `privateKey` is the text of an OpenSSH private key (`-----BEGIN OPENSSH PRIVATE KEY-----`), pasted or read from the file the owner picked; its public key (with the key's own comment) and fingerprint (`SHA256:…`, as `ssh-keygen -l` writes it) are worked out on the server. `generate: true` makes a new ed25519 key without a passphrase, with the comment `botdot:<name>`. The private key is never answered or shown again: show the answer's `publicKey` with a copy button, so the owner can add it to a server's `authorized_keys`. `400` with a sentence to show in the form when there is no name, when the text is not an SSH private key (a public key, a damaged one), when it is another format (an RSA or PKCS#8 PEM, a PuTTY `.ppk`: the sentence says how to convert), when it has a passphrase, or when Bitwarden is not connected; `423` while Bitwarden is locked. Nothing is saved then. Without the feature, show no way to add an SSH key. With `chief-ssh-key-add` the Chief's tools send it too (`create_ssh_key`, #181), as `{ name, allowedBots? }` alone: botdot makes a new key as for `generate: true`, and the answer is the same item. The Chief's call is refused `403` in a turn the owner did not start, `400` with `privateKey` (a key never goes through the Chief's chat), without a name or with an `allowedBots` id that is no bot, `400` without Bitwarden and `423` while it is locked, each with the sentence the Chief passes on; nothing is made then. In a Chief chat that read the web it waits on a card (The Chief reads the web), titled “Make the SSH key “…””, once it passed the refusals before Bitwarden's: a refused call is never a card. The apps see nothing new but the new key in the list.
- With `chief-ssh-key-change` the Chief's tools send `PUT /api/vault/:id` too (`update_vault_item`, #182), for an item of kind `ssh-key` only, with `name`, `add` and `remove` alone (`add` and `remove`: bot ids, applied to the key's bots as they are when the change is done, so a change held on a card keeps what the owner changed meanwhile): the key is renamed in Bitwarden with its key and everything else of it as they were, and its bots are kept beside it as the owner's change keeps them. The owner's own `PUT /api/vault/:id` with `name` renames an SSH key the same way. `200` with the item as the list shows it. The Chief's call is refused `403` in a turn the owner did not start, `400` for an item that is no SSH key (a login, a token, a card: they stay the owner's), an empty name, an `add` or `remove` that is not a list or names no bot, and a call with none of the three, `404` for an id the Vault does not have, `400` without Bitwarden and `423` while it is locked, each with the sentence the Chief passes on; nothing changes then. In a Chief chat that read the web it waits on a card titled “Change the SSH key “…””, once it passed those refusals. The apps see nothing new but the item as it changed.
- `POST /api/vault/files` with `{ fileName, content, name?, allowedBots? }`, when `features` has `vault-files`: a new file in the bot-dot folder; `201` with the item as the list shows it (`kind: "file"`, `fileName`, `size`). `content` is the file's bytes in base64, read in the app from the file the owner picked; `fileName` is its name there, kept with any character but letters, digits, `.`, `-` and `_` made `_` (a dotfile such as `.env` keeps its name; `.` and `..` become `file`); `name` is what the list calls it, the file name when it is empty. The file may be of any type (a key, a certificate, a `.conf`, a `.env`, a binary file, one without an extension, #231) and at most 64 KB: check the size in the app before sending. The content is never answered or shown again. `400` with a sentence to show in the form when no file is given, when it is empty, bigger than 64 KB (the sentence names the limit) or not base64, or when Bitwarden is not connected; `423` while Bitwarden is locked. Nothing is saved then. Without the feature, show no way to add a file.
- `DELETE /api/vault/:id`: `{ ok }`. With Bitwarden the item goes to Bitwarden's trash.
- A refused save is `400` with a sentence to show in the form as it is: "Website is required", "Password is required", or, for a `type` that is given and is not one of the four, "Type must be login, api-token, other or github-app"; for a `github-app`, the sentence about its App ID or key. Nothing is saved, and a refused change keeps nothing else of that request either. Keep the form open with what was typed, and clear the secret.
- An app from before `vault-types` sends no `type`: its new items are logins and the items it changes keep their type. To a server without `vault-types`, send no `type` and show no Type control.

## Things that need the owner

**Decline**, when `features` has `decline-words`. The no on a job or a tile that was never approved removes it, and the server says so in its own words, the same in every app:

- A waiting job (`approved: false`, in a bot's `jobs` and in `GET /api/bots/:id/jobs`), a waiting tile (each of `pending` in `GET /api/dashboard`; with `staged-changes` the tile says it wherever it is sent) and their entries in `approvals.jobs` and `approvals.tiles` of the feed have `no: "Decline and remove"`: the label of the no button, on the card, in the chat and in the notification. Ask nothing before sending it. A job or tile without `no` keeps the app's own word.
- The answer of that no (`POST …/approve` with `{ approve: false }`) has `text: "Declined and removed"`: what the card reads afterwards. Its other fields are as before, `{ ok: true, removed: true }` for a job and `{ ok }` for a tile.

**A change that waits**, when `features` has `staged-changes` ([ADR 013](adr/013-a-change-to-an-approved-job-waits-beside-it.md)). A change to what an approved job does, or to the scripts of an approved tile, that needs the owner's OK does not replace it. It waits beside it, and the approved one keeps running until the owner answers.
