The apps see nothing new. `POST /api/vault/session/computer/place-secret-file` with `{ entries, template?, path, mode?, owner?, host?, sshKey?, reason }` is a bot's `place_secret_file` (#253): `entries` 1 to 8 names of logins and files shared with the bot; `template` the file's text with `{{NAME.username}}`, `{{NAME.password}}` and `{{NAME.file}}`, `NAME` an entry's name, every entry used (without it, one entry alone is the whole file); `path` absolute, of letters, digits and `. _ - @ + = ,` with no `.` or `..` part; `mode` octal, `600` by default; `owner` `user` or `user:group`, `box` by default on the account's computer and none (the SSH user) on another server; `host` `user@server`, reached over SSH from the account's computer with `sshKey`, a saved SSH key shared with the bot. Without the owner's yes for that place it adds a card (see Secrets a bot asks to keep in a file) and answers `{ ok: true, asked: true, text }`. With it, botdot writes the file whole (a temporary file beside it, moved over it) as root on the account's computer, or as the SSH user on the server with the bytes in base64 in the docker CLI's environment, and answers `{ ok: true, text }`. Refused with `{ ok: false, error }`: a bot without a computer, a malformed request, an entry not shared with the bot or neither a login nor a file, a template that names no entry of the list or leaves one out, a file over 64 KB, and a place the owner said no to within the week; what failed comes back with every secret blanked out. The apps see the card and the Vault's `placedIn`. `POST /api/vault/session/computer/shell` with `{ command, timeoutSeconds? }`, `…/read` with `{ path }` and `…/write` with `{ path, content }` are a bot's `run_shell`, `read_file` and `write_file` (#134): a `docker exec` as the bot user in its own folder, with no screen. They start the account's computer if it is off and never the bot's screen, which starts with its first tool that needs one (a screenshot, a click, `start_program`, `open_browser`), as does `POST /api/vault/session/computer` now. `shell` answers as `run-with-login` does, with nothing to hide; `read` answers `{ ok: true, content }` (at most 1 MB of the file) and `write` `{ ok: true }` (`content` at most 900 KB as JSON), each `{ ok: false, error }` when the file can't be read or written. A relative `path` is in the bot's folder. The apps see nothing new. `POST /api/vault/session/computer/browser` with `{ op, ref?, text?, key?, url?, seconds? }` is a bot's browser tools (#170): `op` `snapshot`, `click`, `fill`, `press`, `goto` or `wait` (its `browser_snapshot`, `browser_click`, `browser_fill`, `browser_press`, `browser_goto` and `browser_wait_for`), handed to the browser bridge of the bot's own screen as a saved login is, and run there on the browser's thread. It starts the bot's screen as its other screen tools do. It answers `{ ok: true, snapshot }`, the focused page's accessibility tree as text with a ref on each control (`button "Log in" [ref=e12]`) and no value of a password, card or two-step code box, cut at 12000 characters; a ref is good until the next snapshot, which every answer is. It answers `{ ok: false, error }` for a ref that is unknown or stale, a `fill` of a password, card or two-step code box (the error sends the bot to `type_login`, `type_card` or `type_identity`), a `goto` to anything but http or https, a closed Camoufox and a computer older than the browser tools. `seconds` is how long `wait` waits for `text` or `ref`, 10 by default and 15 at most. The apps see nothing new. `GET /api/vault/session/logins` is a bot's `list_logins`: `{ logins, notShared }`, `logins` the entries shared with the bot as before and `notShared` every other entry it may see but not use (not one that asks for the master password again): a name, and for a login its `type` and `site`, no username, card digits or email. The bot's tool lists both, the second with `shared: false`; to use one, the bot asks with `request_login` and the owner allows it. The apps see nothing new. `POST /api/vault/session/switch` with `{ login, switch, reason }` is a bot's `ask_vault_switch`: it adds a card that asks the owner to turn on `switch` (`insecureTls` or `inComputer`) of `login`, a login shared with the bot (see A switch of a saved login), keeping the run so that the bot carries on after a yes. It answers `{ ok: true, name, login }`, and `{ ok: false, error }` for another switch, a login not shared with the bot or unknown, a switch that is on already, or one the owner said no to for this bot and login within the week; no card is added then. Asked again while its card waits, the card stays one. It answers nothing: only the owner's `POST /api/asks/:id` turns the switch on. A bot's `api_request` to a server whose certificate is not trusted, with a login whose `insecureTls` is off, is told to ask with `ask_vault_switch`. `POST /api/self/session/tool` with `{ name, reason }` is a bot asking the Chief for a tool: it starts a Chief turn and adds no ask itself. `POST /api/self/session/app` with `{ app, reason }` is a bot asking the Chief for a connected app (a Composio slug, e.g. `googlecalendar`) in the same way. A login request (`POST /api/vault/session/request`, and the Chief's `POST /api/vault/requests`) for the site of an app botdot connects (Google Calendar, Gmail, Slack, GitHub, Notion…, `lib/connected-apps.js`) is refused with `{ ok: false, error }` naming the app, unless its `type` is `api-token` or `github-app`; no request is opened, so the apps see nothing new. `POST /api/self/session/file` answers `replyPrivate: true` beside `file` in a scheduled run whose final reply stays private (a `report-only` job that is not a one-time one), so the bot's tool can say that the file needs a report. The Chief's tools send `X-Chief-Chat`, the id of the Chief chat they run for, with every call: it says where the Chief is told of the owner's answer on what the call leaves waiting, and nothing else. It is read only for a call that is the Chief's, and a chat that does not exist counts as none. `GET /api/computer/status` (the same as `GET /api/bots/:id/computer/status`), `GET /api/bots/:id/computer/logs?lines=` and `POST /api/computer/restart` are the Chief's `computer_status`, `computer_logs` and `restart_computer` (#59, #109), for the account's computer when it fails: `status` is Docker's state of it (`state`, `status`, `exitCode`, `error`, `oomKilled`, `startedAt`, `finishedAt`, `restartCount`), `lastStartError: { at, message }`, why its last start failed, `null` once one succeeds, and `usage` as in What each bot uses of it; `logs` is `{ state, container, desktop }`, the last `lines` (1 to 500, 100 by default) of the container's own log and of the bot's screen's supervisord log, not the browser bridge's; `restart` is as under The account's computer. Each goes through the account's Docker helper, so they reach this account's computer only (ADR 028). The apps see nothing new but the Chief's chip "Restarted the account's computer".
